```
1→"""
     2→Clip validation and frame calculation for join operations.
     3→
     4→Validates that clips have sufficient frames for safe joining
     5→and calculates minimum frame requirements.
     6→"""
     7→
     8→from pathlib import Path
     9→from typing import Tuple, List
    10→
    11→from source.utils import download_video_if_url, get_video_frame_count_and_fps
    12→from source.core.log import task_logger
    13→
    14→__all__ = [
    15→    "calculate_min_clip_frames",
    16→    "validate_clip_frames_for_join",
    17→]
    18→
    19→def calculate_min_clip_frames(gap_frame_count: int, context_frame_count: int, replace_mode: bool) -> int:
    20→    """
    21→    Calculate the minimum number of frames a clip must have to safely join.
    22→
    23→    In REPLACE mode, we need:
    24→        gap_frame_count + 2 * context_frame_count <= min_clip_frames
    25→
    26→    This ensures that context frames don't overlap with previously blended regions
    27→    in chained joins, avoiding the "double-blending" artifact.
    28→
    29→    Args:
    30→        gap_frame_count: Number of frames in the generated gap/transition
    31→        context_frame_count: Number of context frames from each clip boundary
    32→        replace_mode: Whether REPLACE mode is enabled
    33→
    34→    Returns:
    35→        Minimum required frames for each clip
    36→    """
    37→    if replace_mode:
    38→        return gap_frame_count + 2 * context_frame_count
    39→    else:
    40→        return 2 * context_frame_count
    41→
    42→def validate_clip_frames_for_join(
    43→    clip_list: List[dict],
    44→    gap_frame_count: int,
    45→    context_frame_count: int,
    46→    replace_mode: bool,
    47→    temp_dir: Path,
    48→    orchestrator_task_id: str,
    49→    **_kwargs
    50→) -> Tuple[bool, str, List[int]]:
    51→    """
    52→    Validate that all clips have enough frames for safe joining.
    53→
    54→    Args:
    55→        clip_list: List of clip dicts with 'url' keys
    56→        gap_frame_count: Gap frames for transition
    57→        context_frame_count: Context frames from each boundary
    58→        replace_mode: Whether REPLACE mode is enabled
    59→        temp_dir: Directory to download clips for frame counting
    60→        orchestrator_task_id: Task ID for logging
    61→
    62→    Returns:
    63→        Tuple of (is_valid, error_message, frame_counts_per_clip)
    64→    """
    65→    min_frames = calculate_min_clip_frames(gap_frame_count, context_frame_count, replace_mode)
    66→    task_logger.debug(f"[VALIDATE_CLIPS] Minimum required frames per clip: {min_frames}")
    67→    task_logger.debug(f"[VALIDATE_CLIPS]   (gap={gap_frame_count} + 2\u00d7context={context_frame_count}, replace_mode={replace_mode})")
    68→
    69→    frame_counts = []
    70→    violations = []
    71→
    72→    for idx, clip in enumerate(clip_list):
    73→        clip_url = clip.get("url")
    74→        if not clip_url:
    75→            return False, f"Clip {idx} missing 'url' field", []
    76→
    77→        # Download clip to count frames
    78→        local_path = download_video_if_url(
    79→            clip_url,
    80→            download_target_dir=temp_dir,
    81→            task_id_for_logging=orchestrator_task_id,
    82→            descriptive_name=f"validate_clip_{idx}"
    83→        )
    84→
    85→        if not local_path or not Path(local_path).exists():
    86→            return False, f"Failed to download clip {idx} for validation: {clip_url}", []
    87→
    88→        # Get frame count
    89→        frames, fps = get_video_frame_count_and_fps(str(local_path))
    90→        if not frames:
    91→            return False, f"Could not determine frame count for clip {idx}", []
    92→
    93→        frame_counts.append(frames)
    94→        task_logger.debug(f"[VALIDATE_CLIPS] Clip {idx}: {frames} frames (min required: {min_frames})")
    95→
    96→        # First and last clips only need half the minimum (only one boundary)
    97→        is_first = (idx == 0)
    98→        is_last = (idx == len(clip_list) - 1)
    99→
   100→        if is_first or is_last:
   101→            if replace_mode:
   102→                gap_from_side = gap_frame_count // 2 if is_first else (gap_frame_count - gap_frame_count // 2)
   103→                required = context_frame_count + gap_from_side
   104→            else:
   105→                required = context_frame_count
   106→        else:
   107→            required = min_frames
   108→
   109→        if frames < required:
   110→            violations.append({
   111→                "idx": idx,
   112→                "frames": frames,
   113→                "required": required,
   114→                "shortfall": required - frames
   115→            })
   116→
   117→    if violations:
   118→        min_available = min(frame_counts)
   119→        total_needed = gap_frame_count + 2 * context_frame_count
   120→        ratio = min_available / total_needed
   121→        reduced_gap = max(1, int(gap_frame_count * ratio))
   122→        reduced_context = max(1, int(context_frame_count * ratio))
   123→
   124→        warning_parts = []
   125→        for v in violations:
   126→            warning_parts.append(f"Clip {v['idx']}: {v['frames']} frames < {v['required']} required")
   127→
   128→        warning_msg = (
   129→            f"[PROPORTIONAL_REDUCTION] Some clips are shorter than ideal:\n  "
   130→            + "\n  ".join(warning_parts)
   131→            + f"\n  Original settings: gap={gap_frame_count}, context={context_frame_count}"
   132→            + f"\n  Will reduce to approximately: gap\u2248{reduced_gap}, context\u2248{reduced_context} ({ratio:.0%} of original)"
   133→            + f"\n  Transitions will be shorter but still generated successfully."
   134→        )
   135→        task_logger.debug(f"[VALIDATE_CLIPS] {warning_msg}")
   136→
   137→        return True, warning_msg, frame_counts
   138→
   139→    task_logger.debug(f"[VALIDATE_CLIPS] All {len(clip_list)} clips have sufficient frames")
   140→    return True, "", frame_counts
   141→
```

```
     7→def run(client, options: dict):
```

```
1→"""Output formatting for debug data."""
     2→
     3→import json
     4→from datetime import datetime, timezone
     5→from typing import Dict, Any, List
     6→from debug.models import (
     7→    TaskInfo, WorkerInfo, TasksSummary, WorkersSummary,
     8→    SystemHealth, OrchestratorStatus
     9→)
    10→
    11→
    12→class Formatter:
    13→    """Output formatting for debug data."""
    14→    
    15→    # ==================== HELPER METHODS ====================
    16→    
    17→    @staticmethod
    18→    def _parse_edge_failure(text: str) -> Dict[str, str] | None:
    19→        """
    20→        Parse standardized edge function failure format.
    21→        
    22→        Format: "[EDGE_FAIL:{function_name}:{error_type}] {details}"
    23→        
    24→        Returns dict with 'function', 'error_type', 'details' or None if not matched.
    25→        """
    26→        import re
    27→        # Match: [EDGE_FAIL:function-name:ERROR_TYPE] details
    28→        match = re.search(r'\[EDGE_FAIL:([^:]+):([^\]]+)\]\s*(.+)', text)
    29→        if match:
    30→            return {
    31→                'function': match.group(1),
    32→                'error_type': match.group(2),
    33→                'details': match.group(3).strip()
    34→            }
    35→        return None
    36→    
    37→    @staticmethod
    38→    def _diagnose_error(text: str) -> List[str]:
    39→        """
    40→        Analyze error text and return diagnosis lines.
    41→        
    42→        Handles:
    43→        - Standardized [EDGE_FAIL:...] format (most reliable)
    44→        - Legacy error patterns (502, timeout, etc.)
    45→        """
    46→        lines = []
    47→        text_lower = text.lower()
    48→        
    49→        # First check for standardized EDGE_FAIL format (most reliable)
    50→        edge_fail = Formatter._parse_edge_failure(text)
    51→        if edge_fail:
    52→            func = edge_fail['function']
    53→            err_type = edge_fail['error_type']
    54→            
    55→            if '5XX' in err_type or err_type in ['HTTP_502', 'HTTP_503', 'HTTP_504']:
    56→                lines.append(f"   🔧 DIAGNOSIS: Edge function '{func}' returned 5xx error (transient)")
    57→                lines.append("   💡 This is usually a transient infrastructure issue")
    58→                if func in ['complete-task', 'storage-upload', 'storage-upload-file']:
    59→                    lines.append("   💡 The video was likely generated successfully but upload failed!")
    60→            elif 'TIMEOUT' in err_type:
```

```
    47→def handle_join_clips_task(

    64→            - model: Optional model override (defaults to wan_2_2_vace_lightning_baseline_2_2_2)
    65→            - resolution: Optional [width, height] override
    66→            - use_input_video_resolution: Optional bool (default False). If True, uses the detected resolution from input video instead of resolution override
    67→            - fps: Optional FPS override (defaults to 16)
    68→            - use_input_video_fps: Optional bool (default False). If True, uses input video's FPS. If False, downsamples to fps param (default 16)
    69→            - max_wait_time: Optional timeout in seconds for generation (defaults to 1800s / 30 minutes)
    70→            - additional_loras: Optional dict of additional LoRAs {name: weight}
```

```
    28→def _extract_boundary_frames_for_vlm(
    29→    clip_list: List[dict],
    30→    temp_dir: Path,
    31→    orchestrator_task_id: str) -> List[Tuple[Optional[str], Optional[str], Optional[str], Optional[str]]]:
    32→    """
    33→    Extract boundary frames from clips for VLM prompt generation.
    34→
    35→    For each join (clip[i] -> clip[i+1]), extracts 4 frames:
    36→    - First frame from clip[i] (scene context)
    37→    - Last frame from clip[i] (boundary)
    38→    - First frame from clip[i+1] (boundary)
    39→    - Last frame from clip[i+1] (scene context)
    40→
    41→    Args:
    42→        clip_list: List of clip dicts with 'url' keys
    43→        temp_dir: Directory to save temporary frame images
    44→        orchestrator_task_id: Task ID for logging
    45→
    46→    Returns:
    47→        List of (start_first, start_boundary, end_boundary, end_last) tuples for each join
    48→    """
    49→    image_quads = []
    50→    num_joins = len(clip_list) - 1
    51→
    52→    # Cache downloaded videos and their extracted frames to avoid re-downloading
    53→    clip_data_cache = {}
    54→
    55→    for idx in range(num_joins):
    56→        clip_start = clip_list[idx]
    57→        clip_end = clip_list[idx + 1]
    58→
    59→        start_url = clip_start.get("url")
    60→        end_url = clip_end.get("url")
    61→
    62→        if not start_url or not end_url:
    63→            orchestrator_logger.debug(f"[VLM_EXTRACT] Join {idx}: Missing URL, skipping")
    64→            image_quads.append((None, None, None, None))
    65→            continue
    66→
    67→        try:
    68→            # === Extract frames from clip_start ===
    69→            if start_url in clip_data_cache:
    70→                start_frames, start_first_path, start_last_path = clip_data_cache[start_url]
    71→            else:
    72→                orchestrator_logger.debug(f"[VLM_EXTRACT] Join {idx}: Downloading clip_start: {start_url[:80]}...")
    73→                local_start_path = download_video_if_url(
    74→                    start_url,
    75→                    download_target_dir=temp_dir,
    76→                    task_id_for_logging=orchestrator_task_id,
    77→                    descriptive_name=f"clip_{idx}_start"

    83→                    image_quads.append((None, None, None, None))
    84→                    continue
    85→
    86→                start_first_path = temp_dir / f"vlm_clip{idx}_first.jpg"
    87→                start_last_path = temp_dir / f"vlm_clip{idx}_last.jpg"
    88→                cv2.imwrite(str(start_first_path), cv2.cvtColor(start_frames[0], cv2.COLOR_RGB2BGR))
    89→                cv2.imwrite(str(start_last_path), cv2.cvtColor(start_frames[-1], cv2.COLOR_RGB2BGR))
    90→
    91→                clip_data_cache[start_url] = (start_frames, str(start_first_path), str(start_last_path))
    92→                orchestrator_logger.debug(f"[VLM_EXTRACT] Join {idx}: Extracted {len(start_frames)} frames from start clip")
    93→
    94→            start_boundary_idx = len(start_frames) - 1
    95→            start_boundary_path = temp_dir / f"vlm_clip{idx}_boundary.jpg"
    96→            cv2.imwrite(str(start_boundary_path), cv2.cvtColor(start_frames[start_boundary_idx], cv2.COLOR_RGB2BGR))
    97→
    98→            # === Extract frames from clip_end ===
    99→            if end_url in clip_data_cache:
   100→                end_frames, end_first_path, end_last_path = clip_data_cache[end_url]
   101→            else:
   102→                orchestrator_logger.debug(f"[VLM_EXTRACT] Join {idx}: Downloading clip_end: {end_url[:80]}...")
   103→                local_end_path = download_video_if_url(
   104→                    end_url,
   105→                    download_target_dir=temp_dir,
   106→                    task_id_for_logging=orchestrator_task_id,
   107→                    descriptive_name=f"clip_{idx+1}_end"
   108→                )
```

```
1→"""
     2→VACE quantization calculations for join clips tasks.
     3→
     4→Handles the 4n+1 frame count constraint that VACE requires, adjusting
     5→gap frame counts to ensure valid generation parameters.
     6→"""
     7→
     8→
     9→def _calculate_vace_quantization(
    10→    context_frame_count: int,
    11→    gap_frame_count: int,
    12→    replace_mode: bool,
    13→    context_before: int = None,
    14→    context_after: int = None
    15→) -> dict:
    16→    """
    17→    Calculate VACE quantization adjustments for frame counts.
    18→
    19→    VACE requires frame counts to match the pattern 4n+1 (e.g., 45, 49, 53).
    20→    When we request a frame count that doesn't match this pattern, VACE will
    21→    quantize down to the nearest valid count.
    22→
    23→    Args:
    24→        context_frame_count: Number of frames from each clip's boundary (symmetric default)
    25→        gap_frame_count: Number of frames to generate in the gap
    26→        replace_mode: Whether we're replacing a portion (True) or inserting (False)
    27→                     Both modes now work similarly for VACE - context + gap + context
    28→        context_before: Override for context frames before gap (for asymmetric cases)
    29→        context_after: Override for context frames after gap (for asymmetric cases)
    30→
    31→    Returns:
    32→        dict with:
    33→            - total_frames: Actual frame count VACE will generate
    34→            - gap_for_guide: Adjusted gap to use in guide/mask creation
    35→            - quantization_shift: Number of frames dropped by VACE (0 if no quantization)
    36→    """
    37→    # Support asymmetric context counts
    38→    ctx_before = context_before if context_before is not None else context_frame_count
    39→    ctx_after = context_after if context_after is not None else context_frame_count
    40→
    41→    # Calculate desired total: context_before + gap + context_after
    42→    desired_total = ctx_before + gap_frame_count + ctx_after
    43→
    44→    # Apply VACE quantization (4n + 1)
    45→    actual_total = ((desired_total - 1) // 4) * 4 + 1
    46→    quantization_shift = desired_total - actual_total
    47→
    48→    # Adjust gap to account for dropped frames
    49→    gap_for_guide = max(0, gap_frame_count - quantization_shift)
    50→
    51→    return {
    52→        'total_frames': actual_total,
    53→        'gap_for_guide': gap_for_guide,
    54→        'quantization_shift': quantization_shift,
    55→        'context_before': ctx_before,
    56→        'context_after': ctx_after,
    57→    }
    58→
```

```
1→"""RunPod sync command."""
     2→
     3→import os
     4→import sys
     5→
     6→import httpx
     7→from dotenv import load_dotenv
     8→
     9→
    10→def run(client, options: dict):
    11→    """Handle 'debug.py runpod' command."""
    12→    load_dotenv()
    13→    
    14→    try:
    15→        import runpod
    16→    except ImportError:
    17→        print("❌ Error: runpod module not installed")
    18→        print("   Install with: pip install runpod")
    19→        sys.exit(1)
    20→    
    21→    runpod_api_key = os.getenv('RUNPOD_API_KEY')
    22→    if not runpod_api_key:
    23→        print("❌ Error: RunPod auth key not set in environment")
    24→        sys.exit(1)
    25→    
    26→    runpod.api_key = runpod_api_key
    27→    
    28→    try:
    29→        # Get RunPod pods
    30→        print("🔍 Analyzing RunPod vs Database state...")
    31→        runpod_pods = runpod.get_pods()
    32→        
    33→        # Filter for active pods that are orchestrator-managed (start with gpu_ or gpu-)
    34→        active_runpod_pods = [
    35→            pod for pod in runpod_pods 
    36→            if pod.get('desiredStatus') in ['RUNNING', 'PROVISIONING']
    37→            and (pod.get('name', '').startswith('gpu_') or pod.get('name', '').startswith('gpu-'))
    38→        ]
    39→        
    40→        # Get database workers (only active/spawning to avoid fetching thousands of terminated workers)
    41→        active_result = client.supabase.table('workers').select('*').eq('status', 'active').execute()
    42→        spawning_result = client.supabase.table('workers').select('*').eq('status', 'spawning').execute()
    43→        
    44→        active_db_workers = (active_result.data or []) + (spawning_result.data or [])
    45→        
    46→        # Create lookup
    47→        db_runpod_ids = set()
    48→        for worker in active_db_workers:
    49→            runpod_id = worker.get('metadata', {}).get('runpod_id')
    50→            if runpod_id:
    51→                db_runpod_ids.add(runpod_id)
    52→        
    53→        # Find orphaned pods
    54→        orphaned_pods = []
    55→        runpod_pod_ids = set()
    56→        
    57→        for pod in active_runpod_pods:
    58→            pod_id = pod.get('id')
    59→            runpod_pod_ids.add(pod_id)
    60→            
    61→            if pod_id not in db_runpod_ids:
    62→                orphaned_pods.append(pod)
    63→        
    64→        # Find database-only workers
    65→        db_only_workers = []
    66→        for worker in active_db_workers:
    67→            runpod_id = worker.get('metadata', {}).get('runpod_id')
    68→            if runpod_id and runpod_id not in runpod_pod_ids:
    69→                db_only_workers.append(worker)
    70→        
    71→        # Report
    72→        print("\n" + "=" * 80)
    73→        print("☁️  RUNPOD SYNC STATUS")
    74→        print("=" * 80)
    75→        
    76→        print(f"\n📊 Summary:")
    77→        print(f"   RunPod active pods: {len(active_runpod_pods)}")
    78→        print(f"   Database active workers: {len(active_db_workers)}")
    79→        print(f"   Orphaned pods (RunPod only): {len(orphaned_pods)}")
    80→        print(f"   Stale workers (DB only): {len(db_only_workers)}")
```

```
    57→# ─── Coordination dataclasses for handle_travel_segment_via_queue ────────────
    58→
    59→@dataclass
    60→class SegmentContext:
    61→    """Mode, orchestrator details, and segment identity."""
    62→    mode: str  # "orchestrator" or "standalone"
    63→    orchestrator_details: dict
    64→    individual_params: dict
    65→    segment_idx: int
    66→    segment_params: dict  # alias for the top-level task_params_dict
    67→    orchestrator_task_id_ref: Optional[str] = None
    68→    orchestrator_run_id: Optional[str] = None
    69→
    70→@dataclass
    71→class GenerationInputs:
    72→    """Model, prompt, resolution, frames, and flags."""
    73→    model_name: str
    74→    prompt_for_wgp: str
    75→    negative_prompt_for_wgp: str
    76→    parsed_res_wh: Tuple[int, int]

    78→    current_run_base_output_dir: Path
    79→    segment_processing_dir: Path

    81→    travel_mode: str
    82→
    83→@dataclass
    84→class ImageRefs:
    85→    """Image reference paths for the segment."""
    86→    start_ref_path: Optional[str] = None
    87→    end_ref_path: Optional[str] = None
    88→    svi_predecessor_video_for_source: Optional[str] = None
    89→    use_svi: bool = False
    90→    is_continuing: bool = False
    91→
    92→@dataclass
    93→class StructureOutputs:
    94→    """Results from structure guidance processing."""
    95→    guide_video_path: Optional[str] = None
    96→    mask_video_path_for_wgp: Optional[Path] = None
    97→    video_prompt_type_str: Optional[str] = None
    98→    structure_config: Optional[StructureGuidanceConfig] = None
    99→
   100→
   101→_MISSING = object()
   102→
   103→def _get_param(key, *sources, default=_MISSING, prefer_truthy: bool = False):
   104→    """
   105→    Get a parameter from multiple sources with precedence (first source wins).
   106→
   107→    By default, only skips None values.
   108→    If prefer_truthy=True, also skips falsy non-bool values (e.g. "", {}, [], 0),
   109→    matching historical `a or b` fallback semantics while still preserving explicit booleans.
   110→    """
   111→    for source in sources:
   112→        if not source or key not in source:
   113→            continue
   114→
   115→        value = source[key]
   116→        if value is None:
   117→            continue
   118→
   119→        if prefer_truthy and not isinstance(value, bool) and not value:
   120→            continue
   121→
   122→        return value
   123→
   124→    return None if default is _MISSING else default
   125→
   126→def _resolve_segment_context(task_params_dict: dict, is_standalone: bool, task_id: str) -> SegmentContext:
   127→    """Resolve mode, orchestrator_details, individual_params, and segment identity.
   128→
   129→    Handles orchestrator vs standalone mode, fetching orchestrator_details from the DB
   130→    if not provided inline.
   131→
   132→    Raises ValueError if required fields are missing.
   133→    """
   134→    segment_params = task_params_dict
   135→    orchestrator_task_id_ref = segment_params.get("orchestrator_task_id_ref")
   136→    orchestrator_run_id = segment_params.get("orchestrator_run_id")
   137→    segment_idx = segment_params.get("segment_index")
   138→
   139→    # Get orchestrator_details (canonical name) or full_orchestrator_payload (legacy alias)
   140→    orchestrator_details: dict = segment_params.get("orchestrator_details") or segment_params.get("full_orchestrator_payload")
   141→
   142→    if is_standalone:
   143→        # Standalone mode: use provided payload, default segment_idx to 0
   144→        if segment_idx is None:
   145→            segment_idx = 0
   146→        if not orchestrator_details:
   147→            raise ValueError(f"Individual travel segment {task_id} missing orchestrator_details")
   148→        headless_logger.debug(f"Running in standalone mode (individual_travel_segment)", task_id=task_id)
   149→    else:
   150→        # Orchestrator mode: require segment_index and either inline orchestrator_details or ability to fetch
   151→        if segment_idx is None:
   152→            raise ValueError(f"Travel segment {task_id} missing segment_index")
   153→
   154→        # If orchestrator_details not inline, try to fetch from parent task
   155→        if not orchestrator_details:
   156→            if not orchestrator_task_id_ref:
   157→                raise ValueError(f"Travel segment {task_id} missing orchestrator_details and orchestrator_task_id_ref")
   158→            orchestrator_task_raw_params_json = db_ops.get_task_params(orchestrator_task_id_ref)
   159→            if orchestrator_task_raw_params_json:
   160→                fetched_params = json.loads(orchestrator_task_raw_params_json) if isinstance(orchestrator_task_raw_params_json, str) else orchestrator_task_raw_params_json
   161→                orchestrator_details = fetched_params.get("orchestrator_details")
   162→
   163→        if not orchestrator_details:
   164→            raise ValueError(f"Travel segment {task_id}: Could not retrieve orchestrator_details")
   165→
   166→    # individual_segment_params has highest priority for segment-specific overrides
   167→    individual_params: IndividualSegmentParams = segment_params.get("individual_segment_params", {})
   168→
   169→    return SegmentContext(
   170→        mode="standalone" if is_standalone else "orchestrator",
   171→        orchestrator_details=orchestrator_details,
   172→        individual_params=individual_params,
   173→        segment_idx=segment_idx,
   174→        segment_params=segment_params,
   175→        orchestrator_task_id_ref=orchestrator_task_id_ref,
   176→        orchestrator_run_id=orchestrator_run_id,
   177→    )
   178→
   179→
   180→def _resolve_generation_inputs(ctx: SegmentContext, task_id: str, main_output_dir_base: Path) -> GenerationInputs:
   181→    """Resolve model, prompt, resolution, frame count, output directory, and flags.
   182→
   183→    Returns a GenerationInputs dataclass with all resolved values.
   184→    Raises ValueError if resolution format is invalid.
   185→    """
   186→    segment_params = ctx.segment_params
   187→    orchestrator_details = ctx.orchestrator_details
   188→    individual_params = ctx.individual_params
   189→    segment_idx = ctx.segment_idx
   190→
   191→    # Model name: segment > orchestrator (required)
   192→    model_name = segment_params.get("model_name") or orchestrator_details.get("model_name")
   193→    if not model_name:
   194→        raise ValueError(f"Travel segment {task_id}: model_name missing from both segment_params and orchestrator_details")
   195→
   196→    # Prompts: enhanced_prompt preferred > base_prompt fallback
   197→    # Frontend may provide both: enhanced_prompt (AI-enhanced) and base_prompt (user's original)
   198→    enhanced_prompt = _get_param("enhanced_prompt", individual_params, segment_params, default=None)
   199→    base_prompt = _get_param("base_prompt", individual_params, segment_params, default=" ")
   200→
   201→    # Use enhanced_prompt if present and non-empty, otherwise base_prompt
   202→    effective_prompt = enhanced_prompt if enhanced_prompt and enhanced_prompt.strip() else base_prompt
   203→    prompt_for_wgp = ensure_valid_prompt(effective_prompt)
   204→
   205→    if enhanced_prompt and enhanced_prompt.strip():
   206→        task_logger.debug(f"[PROMPT] Task {task_id}: Using enhanced_prompt")
   207→    negative_prompt_for_wgp = ensure_valid_negative_prompt(
   208→        _get_param("negative_prompt", individual_params, segment_params, default=" ")
   209→    )
   210→
   211→    # Resolution: segment > orchestrator
   212→    parsed_res_wh_str = segment_params.get("parsed_resolution_wh") or orchestrator_details.get("parsed_resolution_wh")
   213→    if not parsed_res_wh_str:
   214→        raise ValueError(f"Travel segment {task_id}: parsed_resolution_wh missing from both segment_params and orchestrator_details")
   215→    parsed_res_raw = parse_resolution(parsed_res_wh_str)
   216→    if parsed_res_raw is None:
   217→        raise ValueError(f"Travel segment {task_id}: Invalid resolution format {parsed_res_wh_str}")
   218→    from source.utils.resolution_utils import get_model_grid_size
   219→    model_name = segment_params.get("model_name") or orchestrator_details.get("model_name")
   220→    grid_size = get_model_grid_size(model_name)
   221→    parsed_res_wh = snap_resolution_to_model_grid(parsed_res_raw, grid_size)
   222→
   223→    # Frame count: individual_segment_params.num_frames > top-level num_frames > segment_frames_target > segment_frames_expanded[idx]
   224→    total_frames_for_segment = (
   225→        individual_params.get("num_frames") or
   226→        segment_params.get("num_frames") or
   227→        segment_params.get("segment_frames_target")
   228→    )
   229→    if not total_frames_for_segment:
   230→        segment_frames = orchestrator_details.get("segment_frames_expanded", [])
   231→        if segment_idx < len(segment_frames):
   232→            total_frames_for_segment = segment_frames[segment_idx]
   233→        else:
   234→            raise ValueError(
   235→                f"Travel segment {task_id}: no frame count found (segment_idx={segment_idx}, "
   236→                f"segment_frames_expanded has {len(segment_frames)} entries)"
   237→            )
   238→
   239→    current_run_base_output_dir_str = _get_param(
   240→        "current_run_base_output_dir",
   241→        segment_params,
   242→        default=orchestrator_details.get("main_output_dir_for_run", str(main_output_dir_base.resolve())),
   243→        prefer_truthy=True)
   244→
   245→    # Convert to Path and resolve relative paths against main_output_dir_base
   246→    base_dir_path = Path(current_run_base_output_dir_str)
   247→    if not base_dir_path.is_absolute():
   248→        # Relative path - resolve against main_output_dir_base
   249→        current_run_base_output_dir = main_output_dir_base / base_dir_path
   250→    else:
```

## Debug Utility Summary

I've thoroughly explored the debug package in your project. Here's a complete breakdown:

### Package Location and Structure
The debug utility is located at `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/Headless-Wan2GP/debug/`

**Directory structure:**
```
debug/
├── __main__.py          # Entry point for `python -m debug`
├── __init__.py          # Package initialization
├── cli.py               # Argument parsing and command routing
├── client.py            # DebugClient - unified data access layer
├── models.py            # Data models (TaskInfo, WorkerInfo, etc.)
├── formatters.py        # Output formatting (text/JSON)
├── README.md            # Documentation
└── commands/
    ├── __init__.py
    ├── task.py          # Investigate specific task
    ├── worker.py        # Investigate specific worker
    ├── tasks.py         # Analyze multiple recent tasks
    ├── workers.py       # List recent workers
    ├── health.py        # System health check
    ├── orchestrator.py  # Orchestrator status
    ├── config.py        # Configuration display
    ├── runpod.py        # RunPod sync/orphan detection
    └── storage.py       # Storage volume health
```

---

### How to Use It

**Invocation:**
```bash
python -m debug <command> [options]
```

---

### Available Commands

#### 1. **Task Investigation** - Diagnose why a task failed
```bash
debug.py task <task_id>              # Complete task analysis with logs
debug.py task <task_id> --logs-only  # Just the log timeline
debug.py task <task_id> --json       # JSON output for scripting
```
**What it shows:**
- Complete task state from database (status, error messages, timing)
- Full log timeline from system_logs table
- If task failed due to child task, shows cascaded failure details
- For orchestrator tasks, lists all child tasks (travel segments, stitches, etc.)
- Intelligent error diagnosis with remediation hints

#### 2. **Worker Investigation** - Debug worker issues
```bash
debug.py worker <worker_id>                 # Full worker analysis
debug.py worker <worker_id> --check-logging # Is worker.py running?
debug.py worker <worker_id> --startup       # View initialization logs
debug.py worker <worker_id> --logs-only     # Just the log timeline
debug.py worker <worker_id> --hours N       # Change time window (default: 24h)
```
**What it shows:**
- Worker state and metadata
- All logs for the worker (initialization, heartbeats, task processing)
- Tasks currently assigned to worker
- Pre-termination VRAM info
- Pod status

**Specific use cases:**
- `--check-logging`: Verifies if worker.py actually started (checks if any logs exist). If no logs, worker crashed during initialization or hasn't started yet
- `--startup`: Shows first 100 startup-related logs, detects common issues (ModuleNotFoundError, process death, etc.)

#### 3. **Multi-Task Analysis** - Get task statistics
```bash
debug.py tasks                     # Recent 50 tasks (default)
debug.py tasks --limit 100         # Get different number of tasks
debug.py tasks --status Completed  # Filter by status
debug.py tasks --type travel_segment  # Filter by task type
debug.py tasks --worker <worker_id>   # Filter by worker
debug.py tasks --hours 12          # Filter by time window
```
**What it shows:**
- Task distribution by status and type
- Timing statistics (avg processing time, avg queue time)
- Recent failures with error messages

#### 4. **Multi-Worker Analysis** - Monitor worker fleet
```bash
debug.py workers                    # List recent workers (default: 2 hours)
debug.py workers --hours 24         # Change time window
debug.py workers --detailed         # Show detailed failure analysis
```
**What it shows:**
- Worker status counts (active, spawning, error, terminated)
- Healthy vs stale workers (checks heartbeat freshness)
- Recent failures and failure rates
- Metadata about each worker

#### 5. **System Health Check** - Quick overview
```bash
debug.py health
debug.py health --json
```
**What it shows:**
- Active workers vs spawning
- Healthy workers count
- Queued and in-progress task counts
- Top 10 recent error logs

#### 6. **Orchestrator Status** - Is orchestrator running?
```bash
debug.py orchestrator           # Status of orchestrator GPU
debug.py orchestrator --hours 2 # Check last 2 hours (default: 1h)
```
**What it shows:**
- Last activity time and cycle number
- Status: HEALTHY (< 5 min), WARNING (5-15 min), STALE (> 15 min), or NO_LOGS
- Recent orchestrator cycles with timestamps

#### 7. **Configuration Display** - View timing/scaling settings
```bash
debug.py config              # Show configuration values
debug.py config --explain    # With detailed explanations
```
**What it shows:**
- ORCHESTRATOR_POLL_SEC — How often orchestrator checks workers
- WORKER_GRACE_PERIOD_SEC — Grace period before idle check
- GPU_IDLE_TIMEOUT_SEC — Heartbeat timeout
- GPU_OVERCAPACITY_IDLE_TIMEOUT_SEC — Idle timeout when over capacity
- MIN_ACTIVE_GPUS / MAX_ACTIVE_GPUS — Scaling limits
- TASKS_PER_GPU_THRESHOLD — When to scale up
- RunPod API configuration
- Database configuration
- (With --explain) Worker termination logic and timing calculations

#### 8. **RunPod Sync Check** - Find money leaks
```bash
debug.py runpod                # Find orphaned pods
debug.py runpod --terminate    # Terminate orphaned pods
```
**What it shows:**
- Orphaned pods (running in RunPod but not tracked in database)
- Stale workers (in database but pod terminated)
- Cost analysis (daily/monthly waste)
- Pod details (name, ID, creation time)

#### 9. **Storage Health** - Check volume status
```bash
debug.py storage              # Check storage health
debug.py storage --expand <volume_id>  # Expand volume
```

---

### Global Options

All commands support:
- `--json` — Output as JSON (for scripting/automation)
- `--debug` — Show full traceback on errors

---

### Key Features for Debugging Task Failures

#### Error Diagnosis
The tool includes intelligent error analysis that:
- Parses standardized `[EDGE_FAIL:function:error_type]` format
- Detects 5xx errors (usually transient infrastructure issues)
- Recognizes timeout patterns
- Identifies GPU OOM errors
- Detects LoRA loading failures
- Flags cascaded failures from child tasks

#### Cascaded Failure Tracking
- Automatically follows cascade patterns like "Cascaded failed from related task <uuid>"
- Recursively fetches child task info
- For orchestrator tasks, fetches all related child tasks (travel segments, stitches, etc.)

#### Log-First Approach
All commands query `system_logs` table as primary data source, providing:
- Complete timeline of what actually happened
- Timestamps for debugging timing issues
- Breadcrumb trail of execution

---

### Architecture

**DebugClient** (`client.py`) — Unified data access:
- Connects to Supabase using SUPABASE_URL and SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY
- LogQueryClient sub-client for flexible log queries with filters
- Methods for fetching task info, worker info, system summaries

**Data Models** (`models.py`):
- `TaskInfo` — Task state + logs + optional cascaded/child tasks
- `WorkerInfo` — Worker state + logs + assigned tasks
- `TasksSummary` — Batch task analysis
- `WorkersSummary` — Batch worker analysis
- `SystemHealth` — Overall system metrics
- `OrchestratorStatus` — Orchestrator cycle info

**Formatters** (`formatters.py`):
- Converts data models to human-readable text or JSON
- Includes intelligent error diagnosis
- Highlights critical information with symbols (✅, ❌, ⚠️, etc.)

---

### Environment Requirements

The debug tool requires:
- `SUPABASE_URL` — Supabase project URL
- `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY` — Service role key for admin access
- Optional: `RUNPOD_API_KEY` for RunPod pod management

---

### Common Investigation Scenarios

**"Why did this task fail?"**
```bash
python -m debug task <task_id>
```
Shows complete picture: error message, logs, timing, worker assignment, and root cause analysis

**"Worker isn't processing tasks"**
```bash
python -m debug worker <worker_id> --check-logging
python -m debug worker <worker_id> --startup
```
Verifies if worker started, shows initialization process, detects crashes

**"System seems unhealthy"**
```bash
python -m debug health
python -m debug orchestrator
python -m debug workers
```
Quick overview of worker fleet, task queue, and recent errors

**"We're wasting money on orphaned pods"**
```bash
python -m debug runpod
python -m debug runpod --terminate
```
Finds pods running in RunPod but not tracked in database

**"Why are workers being terminated?"**
```bash
python -m debug config --explain
```
Shows all timing configuration with explanations for idle termination logic

## Summary: Clip Joining Code Architecture in Headless-Wan2GP

I've thoroughly explored the clip joining system in `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/Headless-Wan2GP/source/task_handlers/join/`. Here's my comprehensive analysis:

---

### **1. Directory Structure & Key Files**

The join system is organized in `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/Headless-Wan2GP/source/task_handlers/join/`:

| File | Purpose |
|------|---------|
| **orchestrator.py** | Main entry point (`_handle_join_clips_orchestrator_task`): Parses orchestrator payload, validates clips, optionally runs VLM enhancement, creates child join tasks |
| **generation.py** | Core join generation (`handle_join_clips_task`): Bridges two video clips using VACE generation; handles frame extraction, context preservation, gap generation |
| **final_stitch.py** | Final stitching phase (`handle_join_final_stitch`): Assembles all trimmed clips + transitions into single video with crossfades |
| **task_builder.py** | Task creation utilities: `_create_join_chain_tasks` (legacy sequential) and `_create_parallel_join_tasks` (new parallel pattern) |
| **shared.py** | Orchestrator helpers: idempotency checks, cancellation handling, settings extraction |
| **clip_validator.py** | Pre-join validation: Ensures clips have sufficient frames for safe joining |
| **vlm_enhancement.py** | Optional VLM prompts: Extracts boundary frames and generates context-aware transition prompts using Qwen VLM |
| **vace_quantization.py** | VACE frame count handling: Adjusts frame counts to match VACE's 4n+1 constraint |

---

### **2. Main Flow: How Clip Joining Works**

#### **A. ORCHESTRATOR PHASE** (`orchestrator.py:_handle_join_clips_orchestrator_task`)

The orchestrator is the entry point that orchestrates the entire join workflow:

```
Input: clip_list = [clip_A, clip_B, clip_C, clip_D]
       + orchestrator_details (settings for all joins)

1. PARSE PAYLOAD
   - Extract clip_list, run_id, join_settings, per_join_settings
   - Support dynamic clip_list from segment_task_ids (for travel → join pipelines)
   - Handle loop_first_clip: reverse first clip to create looping effect

2. EARLY IDEMPOTENCY CHECK
   - Look for existing child tasks (both chain and parallel patterns)
   - Return early if tasks already exist and are complete/in-progress

3. VALIDATE CLIP FRAMES
   - Download each clip, count frames
   - Ensure clips have minimum required frames for safe joining
   - REPLACE mode: needs gap_frame_count + 2×context_frame_count per clip
   - INSERT mode: needs 2×context_frame_count per clip
   - Return proportional reduction suggestions if validation fails

4. DETECT RESOLUTION (optional)
   - If use_input_video_resolution=True: detect from first clip
   - Override resolution parameter

5. VLM ENHANCEMENT (optional)
   - If enhance_prompt=True: Extract 4 boundary frames per join
   - Run Qwen VLM to generate context-aware transition prompts
   - Graceful fallback if VLM fails

6. CREATE CHILD TASKS
   - Choose pattern: PARALLEL (default) or CHAIN (legacy)
```

#### **B. PARALLEL PATTERN** (New, Recommended)

```
Creates:
  - N-1 join_clips_segment tasks (transitions) in PARALLEL (no dependencies)
    Each with transition_only=True (only generates transition, no stitching)
  - 1 final_stitch task that depends on ALL transitions

Benefits:
  - No re-encoding: original clips never reprocessed
  - Parallel execution: all transitions run simultaneously
  - Better quality: each transition is fresh generation
  - Proper handling of per-transition parameters
```

#### **C. CHAIN PATTERN** (Legacy)

```
Creates:
  - N-1 join_clips_segment tasks in DEPENDENCY CHAIN
    join_0: clip_A + clip_B → AB.mp4
    join_1: AB.mp4 + clip_C → ABC.mp4 (depends on join_0)
    join_2: ABC.mp4 + clip_D → ABCD.mp4 (depends on join_1)
  - 1 final_stitch task (chain_mode=True, passthrough)

Drawback:
  - Each join re-encodes the accumulated video (quality degradation)
  - Slower: sequential execution
  - DEPRECATED in favor of parallel
```

---

#### **D. JOIN CLIPS SEGMENT PHASE** (`generation.py:handle_join_clips_task`)

Bridges two consecutive video clips using VACE generation:

```
Input: starting_video_path, ending_video_path, context_frame_count, gap_frame_count, ...

STEPS:

1. PARAMETER VALIDATION
   - Check for required paths
   - Handle orchestrator context (fetch predecessor output if needed)
   - Validate task_queue availability

2. VIDEO PREPROCESSING
   a) FPS STANDARDIZATION
      - use_input_video_fps: keep original (True) or downsample to fps param (False)
      - Default: downsample to 16 fps
      - May trigger re-sampling if source is different fps

   b) ASPECT RATIO STANDARDIZATION
      - If aspect_ratio specified: center-crop both videos to match
      - If not specified: auto-detect and standardize ending video to match starting
      - Example: 16:9 vs 9:16 → one will be cropped to match the other

3. FRAME COUNT DETECTION
   - Use OpenCV + ffprobe to count frames in each video
   - Auto-adjust context_frame_count if exceeds available frames
   - Fail if video format unsupported (some WebM codecs fail)

4. VACE QUANTIZATION CALCULATION
   - VACE requires frame counts = 4n+1 (e.g., 45, 49, 53)
   - Adjust gap_frame_count to match quantization
   - Calculate gap split for REPLACE mode (how much to take from each clip)

5. CONTEXT FRAME EXTRACTION
   - Extract last N frames from starting_video
   - Extract first N frames from ending_video
   - In REPLACE mode: context comes from OUTSIDE gap region
   - In INSERT mode: context frames preserved, gap inserted between

6. GUIDE & MASK VIDEO CREATION
   - Create guide video: context1 + gap + context2
   - Create mask video: marks which frames are "generated" vs "preserved"
   - Mask preserves context frames, allows VACE to generate only gap

7. VACE GENERATION
   - Queue generation task with prompt + guide + mask
   - Generate transition frames
   - Handle VLM-enhanced prompts if available

8. OUTPUT MODES:
   - transition_only=True (parallel mode):
     Return JSON: {transition_url, gap_from_clip1, gap_from_clip2, ...}
   - transition_only=False (chain mode):
     Stitch: clip1_trimmed + transition + clip2_trimmed → output video
```

---

#### **E. FINAL STITCH PHASE** (`final_stitch.py:handle_join_final_stitch`)

Assembles all clips and transitions into final seamless video:

```
Input: clip_list, transition_task_ids (from completed join tasks)

STEPS:

1. CHAIN MODE HANDLING (chain_only)
   - Passthrough: return output from last join in chain
   - No re-stitching needed (already done sequentially)
   - Audio already muxed by last join

2. PARALLEL MODE (NEW):
   a) FETCH TRANSITION OUTPUTS
      - Get JSON from each transition_task_id
      - Extract: transition_url, gap_frames, context_from_clip1/2, gap_from_clip1/2
      - Verify structure consistency: frames = ctx1 + gap + ctx2

   b) ALIGNMENT VERIFICATION TABLE
      - Diagnostic output showing exact frame indices for each transition
      - Detects mismatches in clip frame counts vs transition expectations
      - Shows context ranges: [start_idx:end_idx]

   c) PIXEL VERIFICATION (optional but valuable)
      - Extract context frames from original clips
      - Compare pixel-identically with transition context frames
      - Runs offset detection (-2, -1, 0, +1, +2 frames) to detect alignment bugs
      - Logs detailed pixel differences for debugging

   d) DOWNLOAD ALL FILES
      - Download original clips
      - Download all transition videos
      - All done locally for final stitching pass

   e) TRIM CLIPS BASED ON GAP VALUES
      - For each clip:
        trim_start = gap_from_clip2 from PREVIOUS transition
        trim_end = gap_from_clip1 from CURRENT transition
      - First/last clips: 0 trim on outer edges
      - Extract trimmed ranges to temporary videos

   f) BUILD STITCH LIST WITH CROSSFADES
      - Pattern: [clip0] → [trans0] → [clip1] → [trans1] → [clip2]
      - Blend frames: context_from_clip1 (clip→trans), context_from_clip2 (trans→clip)
      - Each crossfade overlaps N frames (per-transition values)

   g) FRAME ACCOUNTING TABLE
      - Log exact frame ranges in final output
      - Shows which clips/transitions contribute which frames
      - Diagnostic for post-stitch validation

   h) STITCH VIDEOS WITH CROSSFADE
      - Concatenate all videos (clips + transitions)
      - Apply linear_sharp crossfades at boundaries
      - Output: seamless joined video

   i) ADD AUDIO (optional)
      - If audio_url provided: mux into final video
      - Graceful fallback if audio fails

   j) VERIFY & UPLOAD
      - Check output exists and is non-empty
      - Count final frames and fps
      - Upload to storage
      - Return final URL
```

---

### **3. Common Failure Modes**

#### **A. Input Validation Failures**

| Failure | Cause | Solution |
|---------|-------|----------|
| `orchestrator_details missing` | Malformed payload | Check orchestrator request structure |
| `clip_list must contain at least 2 clips` | Single clip provided | Provide ≥2 clips |
| `run_id is required` | Missing orchestrator ID | Ensure run_id in payload |
| `Segment task {id} has no output_location` | Segment didn't complete | Wait for segment completion |

#### **B. Clip Validation Failures**

| Failure | Cause | Solution |
|---------|-------|----------|
| `Clip {idx} missing 'url' field` | Malformed clip dict | Each clip needs `{url: "...", name: "..."}` |
| `Failed to download clip {idx}` | Invalid/unreachable URL | Verify URL is accessible |
| `Could not determine frame count` | Unsupported codec (WebM, etc.) | Re-encode to MP4/H.264 |
| `Clips are shorter than ideal` | Insufficient frames | Reduce gap_frame_count or context_frame_count |

#### **C. Video Processing Failures**

| Failure | Cause | Solution |
|---------|-------|----------|
| `Failed to ensure video FPS` | ffmpeg issue | Check ffmpeg installation, codec support |
| `Failed to standardize aspect ratio` | Dimension detection failed | Verify video has valid streams |
| `Could not detect frame count` | OpenCV/ffprobe error | Try different video codec, check file integrity |
| `Extract frame range failed` | ffmpeg frame extraction error | Reduce context_frame_count, check video format |

#### **D. VACE Generation Failures**

| Failure | Cause | Solution |
|---------|-------|----------|
| `Videos too short for requested gap` | Gap + context exceeds available frames | Reduce gap_frame_count or context_frame_count |
| `Starting/Ending video too short` | Specific clip doesn't have enough frames | Use longer source clips, reduce constraints |
| `Generation timeout` | VACE taking >30min | Increase max_wait_time or reduce resolution |
| `Model load failed` | GPU OOM or model not available | Reduce resolution, check GPU memory |

#### **E. Stitching Failures**

| Failure | Cause | Solution |
|---------|-------|----------|
| `Expected N transitions, got M` | Mismatched transition count | Verify all transitions completed successfully |
| `Failed to get output for transition` | Transition task failed/incomplete | Check transition task status |
| `Frame count mismatch` | Clips changed between tasks | Ensure source clips are immutable |
| `Failed to trim clip` | ffmpeg trimming error | Check video format, reduce trim amounts |
| `Failed to stitch videos` | ffmpeg concat error | Check crossfade parameters, codec compatibility |
| `Final output file is empty` | ffmpeg produced no output | Check codec, resolution, fps parameters |

#### **F. Parallel vs Chain Pattern Issues**

| Issue | Pattern | Solution |
|-------|---------|----------|
| Re-encoding artifacts | CHAIN (cumulative quality loss) | Switch to PARALLEL pattern |
| Missing per-join settings | PARALLEL (not propagated) | Verify per_join_settings array |
| Chain dependency broken | CHAIN (if join_i crashes) | Check intermediate video outputs |
| Transition output parsing | PARALLEL (JSON parsing) | Ensure transition outputs are valid JSON |

#### **G. Frame Alignment Issues**

| Issue | Root Cause | Detection |
|-------|-----------|-----------|
| Off-by-one frame shifts | Quantization calculation | PIXEL_CHECK logs show offset ≠ 0 |
| Gap frames mismatch | Different calculation paths | ALIGNMENT TABLE shows inconsistent gap values |
| Context frame corruption | VACE modification | PIXEL_CHECK shows pixel differences >1.0 |
| Trim mismatch | Per-transition gap values differ | Verify all transitions use consistent gap values |

---

### **4. Key Configuration Parameters**

| Parameter | Default | Impact | Notes |
|-----------|---------|--------|-------|
| `gap_frame_count` | 53 | Frames generated in transition | Must result in 4n+1 after quantization |
| `context_frame_count` | 8 | Frames from each clip boundary | Auto-reduced if clips too short |
| `replace_mode` | False | INSERT (False) vs REPLACE (True) | REPLACE removes boundary frames before gap |
| `fps` | 16 | Output video frame rate | Lower = faster gen, higher = more detail |
| `aspect_ratio` | None | Force aspect ratio (e.g., "16:9") | Auto-detects and standardizes if not set |
| `use_input_video_resolution` | False | Use source resolution vs override | Detected from first clip |
| `use_input_video_fps` | False | Keep source fps vs downsample | Default: downsample to fps param |
| `enhance_prompt` | False | Run VLM enhancement | Adds compute but better transition context |
| `use_parallel_joins` | True | Parallel vs Chain pattern | Chain is DEPRECATED, avoid |
| `keep_bridging_images` | False | Preserve intermediate frames | For debugging |

---

### **5. Critical Architectural Decisions**

1. **VACE Quantization (4n+1)**
   - VACE can only generate 4n+1 frames (45, 49, 53, etc.)
   - `_calculate_vace_quantization()` adjusts gap to match nearest valid count
   - Affects gap split in REPLACE mode

2. **Parallel vs Chain**
   - **PARALLEL**: transitions generated independently, stitched once
   - **CHAIN**: each join compounds previous (legacy, avoid)
   - Default now uses PARALLEL + final_stitch

3. **Per-Transition Gap Values**
   - Each transition can have different gap_from_clip1/2
   - Ground truth comes from VACE output, not orchestrator calculation
   - final_stitch uses per-transition values (never orchestrator fallback)

4. **Context Preservation**
   - INSERT mode: context frames untouched, gap inserted
   - REPLACE mode: gap REPLACES boundary frames, context extracted elsewhere
   - Guides + masks ensure VACE respects these constraints

5. **Idempotency Checks**
   - Early check in orchestrator prevents duplicate task creation
   - Handles both chain and parallel patterns
   - Returns status of in-progress tasks

6. **Frame Alignment Verification**
   - final_stitch includes extensive diagnostic logging
   - ALIGNMENT TABLE, FRAME ACCOUNTING, PIXEL_CHECK
   - Helps debug off-by-one and frame shift issues

---

### **6. Entry Points & Task Dispatch**

Registered in `/source/task_handlers/tasks/task_registry.py`:

```python
TASK_HANDLERS = {
    "join_clips_orchestrator": handle_join_clips_orchestrator_task,
    "join_clips_segment": handle_join_clips_task,
    "join_final_stitch": handle_join_final_stitch,
}
```

Flow:
1. `join_clips_orchestrator` task dispatched
2. → Calls orchestrator.py, creates child join tasks
3. → Each `join_clips_segment` task generates transition (parallel)
4. → `join_final_stitch` task waits for all transitions
5. → Stitches final video

---

### **7. Debugging Aids in Logs**

Key log prefixes to search for:

- `[JOIN_ORCHESTRATOR]` - orchestrator decisions
- `[VALIDATE_CLIPS]` - frame count validation
- `[JOIN_CLIPS]` - generation preprocessing + main flow
- `[FINAL_STITCH]` - stitching phase
- `[ALIGNMENT_TABLE]` - frame indices per transition
- `[FRAME_ACCOUNTING]` - final video composition
- `[PIXEL_CHECK]` - context frame verification
- `[OFFSET_DETECT]` - frame shift detection
- `[CANCELLATION]` - orchestrator cancellation propagation

---

This architecture enables robust, parallelizable clip joining with extensive frame-level diagnostics and fallback handling. The parallel pattern avoids quality loss from repeated encoding, while comprehensive validation catches frame alignment issues early.

```
   122→
   123→            clip_list = built_clip_list
   124→            orchestrator_logger.debug(f"[JOIN_ORCHESTRATOR] Built clip_list with {len(clip_list)} clips from segment outputs")
   125→
   126→        if not run_id:
   127→            return False, "run_id is required"
   128→
   129→        # Handle loop_first_clip: reverse the first clip and use it as the second clip
   130→        if loop_first_clip:
   131→            orchestrator_logger.debug(f"[JOIN_ORCHESTRATOR] loop_first_clip=True - will reverse first clip to create looping effect")
   132→
   133→            if not clip_list or len(clip_list) < 1:
   134→                return False, "clip_list must contain at least 1 clip when loop_first_clip=True"
   135→
   136→            loop_temp_dir = Path(main_output_dir_base) / f"join_clips_run_{run_id}" / "loop_temp"
   137→            loop_temp_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
   138→
   139→            first_clip = clip_list[0]
   140→            first_clip_url = first_clip.get("url")
   141→            first_clip_name = first_clip.get("name", "clip_0")
   142→
   143→            if not first_clip_url:
   144→                return False, "First clip in clip_list is missing 'url' field"
   145→
   146→            orchestrator_logger.debug(f"[JOIN_ORCHESTRATOR] Downloading first clip for reversal: {first_clip_url[:80]}...")
   147→            local_first_clip_path = download_video_if_url(
   148→                first_clip_url,
   149→                download_target_dir=loop_temp_dir,
   150→                task_id_for_logging=orchestrator_task_id_str,
   151→                descriptive_name="first_clip_for_loop"
   152→            )
   153→
   154→            if not local_first_clip_path or not Path(local_first_clip_path).exists():
   155→                return False, f"Failed to download first clip: {first_clip_url}"
   156→
   157→            reversed_clip_path = loop_temp_dir / f"{first_clip_name}_reversed.mp4"
   158→            orchestrator_logger.debug(f"[JOIN_ORCHESTRATOR] Reversing first clip...")
   159→
   160→            reversed_path = reverse_video(
   161→                local_first_clip_path,
   162→                reversed_clip_path)
   163→
   164→            if not reversed_path or not reversed_path.exists():
   165→                return False, f"Failed to reverse first clip: {local_first_clip_path}"
   166→
   167→            orchestrator_logger.debug(f"[JOIN_ORCHESTRATOR] \u2705 Created reversed clip locally: {reversed_path}")
   168→
   169→            reversed_filename = f"{first_clip_name}_reversed.mp4"
   170→            reversed_url = upload_intermediate_file_to_storage(
   171→                local_file_path=reversed_path,
   172→                task_id=orchestrator_task_id_str,
   173→                filename=reversed_filename)
   174→
   175→            if not reversed_url:
   176→                orchestrator_logger.debug(f"[JOIN_ORCHESTRATOR] \u26a0\ufe0f  Upload failed, using local path (may fail on multi-worker)")
   177→                reversed_url = str(reversed_path.resolve())
   178→            else:
   179→                orchestrator_logger.debug(f"[JOIN_ORCHESTRATOR] \u2705 Reversed clip uploaded: {reversed_url}")
   180→
   181→            reversed_clip_dict = {
   182→                "url": reversed_url,
   183→                "name": f"{first_clip_name}_reversed"
   184→            }
   185→
   186→            clip_list = [first_clip, reversed_clip_dict]
   187→            orchestrator_logger.debug(f"[JOIN_ORCHESTRATOR] clip_list overridden: [{first_clip_name}, {reversed_clip_dict['name']}]")
   188→
   189→        if not clip_list or len(clip_list) < 2:
   190→            return False, "clip_list must contain at least 2 clips"
   191→
   192→        num_joins = len(clip_list) - 1
   193→        orchestrator_logger.debug(f"[JOIN_ORCHESTRATOR] Processing {len(clip_list)} clips = {num_joins} join tasks")
   194→
   195→        # === EARLY IDEMPOTENCY CHECK (before expensive VLM work) ===
   196→        idempotency_check = _check_existing_join_tasks(orchestrator_task_id_str, num_joins)
   197→        if idempotency_check is not None:
   198→            return idempotency_check
   199→
   200→        # Extract join settings using shared helper
   201→        join_settings = _extract_join_settings_from_payload(orchestrator_payload)
   202→        per_join_settings = orchestrator_payload.get("per_join_settings", [])
   203→        output_base_dir = orchestrator_payload.get("output_base_dir", str(main_output_dir_base.resolve()))
   204→
   205→        # Create run-specific output directory
   206→        current_run_output_dir = Path(output_base_dir) / f"join_clips_run_{run_id}"
   207→        current_run_output_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
   208→        orchestrator_logger.debug(f"[JOIN_ORCHESTRATOR] Run output directory: {current_run_output_dir}")
   209→
   210→        # === VALIDATE CLIP FRAME COUNTS (optional, enabled by default) ===
   211→        skip_validation = orchestrator_payload.get("skip_frame_validation", False)
   212→        if not skip_validation:
   213→            validation_temp_dir = current_run_output_dir / "validation_temp"
   214→            validation_temp_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
   215→
   216→            is_valid, validation_message, frame_counts = validate_clip_frames_for_join(
   217→                clip_list=clip_list,
   218→                gap_frame_count=join_settings.get("gap_frame_count", 53),
   219→                context_frame_count=join_settings.get("context_frame_count", 8),
   220→                replace_mode=join_settings.get("replace_mode", False),
   221→                temp_dir=validation_temp_dir,
   222→                orchestrator_task_id=orchestrator_task_id_str)
   223→
   224→            if not is_valid:
   225→                orchestrator_logger.debug(f"[JOIN_ORCHESTRATOR] VALIDATION FAILED: {validation_message}")
   226→                return False, f"Clip frame validation failed: {validation_message}"
   227→
   228→            if validation_message:
   229→                orchestrator_logger.debug(f"[JOIN_ORCHESTRATOR] {validation_message}")
   230→
   231→            orchestrator_logger.debug(f"[JOIN_ORCHESTRATOR] Clip frame validation complete, frame counts: {frame_counts}")
   232→        else:
   233→            orchestrator_logger.debug(f"[JOIN_ORCHESTRATOR] Frame validation skipped (skip_frame_validation=True)")
   234→
   235→        # === DETECT RESOLUTION FROM INPUT VIDEO (when use_input_video_resolution=True) ===
   236→        if join_settings.get("use_input_video_resolution", False):
   237→            orchestrator_logger.debug(f"[JOIN_ORCHESTRATOR] use_input_video_resolution=True, detecting resolution from first clip...")
   238→
   239→            first_clip_url = clip_list[0].get("url")
   240→            if first_clip_url:
   241→                resolution_temp_dir = current_run_output_dir / "resolution_temp"
   242→                resolution_temp_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
   243→
   244→                local_first_clip = download_video_if_url(
   245→                    first_clip_url,
   246→                    download_target_dir=resolution_temp_dir,
   247→                    task_id_for_logging=orchestrator_task_id_str,
   248→                    descriptive_name="detect_resolution"
   249→                )
   250→
   251→                if local_first_clip and Path(local_first_clip).exists():
   252→                    detected_res = _get_video_resolution(local_first_clip)
   253→                    if detected_res:
   254→                        join_settings["resolution"] = list(detected_res)
   255→                        orchestrator_logger.debug(f"[JOIN_ORCHESTRATOR] \u2713 Detected resolution from input video: {detected_res}")
   256→                    else:
   257→                        orchestrator_logger.debug(f"[JOIN_ORCHESTRATOR] \u26a0 Could not detect resolution, segments will detect from frames")
   258→                else:
   259→                    orchestrator_logger.debug(f"[JOIN_ORCHESTRATOR] \u26a0 Could not download first clip for resolution detection")
   260→
   261→        # === VLM PROMPT ENHANCEMENT (optional) ===
   262→        enhance_prompt = orchestrator_payload.get("enhance_prompt", False)
   263→        vlm_enhanced_prompts: List[Optional[str]] = [None] * num_joins
   264→
   265→        if enhance_prompt:
   266→            orchestrator_logger.debug(f"[JOIN_ORCHESTRATOR] enhance_prompt=True, generating VLM-enhanced prompts for {num_joins} joins")
   267→
   268→            vlm_device = orchestrator_payload.get("vlm_device", "cuda")
   269→            vlm_temp_dir = current_run_output_dir / "vlm_temp"
   270→            vlm_temp_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
   271→            base_prompt = join_settings.get("prompt", "")
   272→
   273→            try:
   274→                orchestrator_logger.debug(f"[JOIN_ORCHESTRATOR] Extracting 4 frames per join from {len(clip_list)} clips...")
   275→                image_quads = _extract_boundary_frames_for_vlm(
   276→                    clip_list=clip_list,
   277→                    temp_dir=vlm_temp_dir,
   278→                    orchestrator_task_id=orchestrator_task_id_str)
   279→
   280→                orchestrator_logger.debug(f"[JOIN_ORCHESTRATOR] Running VLM batch on {len(image_quads)} quads (4 images each)...")
   281→                vlm_enhanced_prompts = _generate_vlm_prompts_for_joins(
   282→                    image_quads=image_quads,
   283→                    base_prompt=base_prompt,
   284→                    vlm_device=vlm_device)
   285→
   286→                valid_count = sum(1 for p in vlm_enhanced_prompts if p is not None)
   287→                orchestrator_logger.debug(f"[JOIN_ORCHESTRATOR] VLM enhancement complete: {valid_count}/{num_joins} prompts generated")
   288→
   289→            except (RuntimeError, ValueError, OSError) as vlm_error:
   290→                orchestrator_logger.debug(f"[JOIN_ORCHESTRATOR] VLM enhancement failed, using base prompts: {vlm_error}", exc_info=True)
   291→                vlm_enhanced_prompts = [None] * num_joins
   292→        else:
   293→            orchestrator_logger.debug(f"[JOIN_ORCHESTRATOR] enhance_prompt=False, using base prompt for all joins")
   294→
   295→        # === CREATE JOIN TASKS ===
   296→        use_parallel = orchestrator_payload.get("use_parallel_joins", True)
   297→
   298→        parent_generation_id = (
   299→            task_params_from_db.get("parent_generation_id")
   300→            or orchestrator_payload.get("parent_generation_id")
   301→        )
   302→
   303→        if use_parallel:
   304→            orchestrator_logger.debug(f"[JOIN_ORCHESTRATOR] Using PARALLEL pattern (transitions in parallel + final stitch)")
   305→            success, message = _create_parallel_join_tasks(
   306→                clip_list=clip_list,
   307→                run_id=run_id,
   308→                join_settings=join_settings,
   309→                per_join_settings=per_join_settings,
   310→                vlm_enhanced_prompts=vlm_enhanced_prompts,
   311→                current_run_output_dir=current_run_output_dir,
   312→                orchestrator_task_id_str=orchestrator_task_id_str,
   313→                orchestrator_project_id=orchestrator_project_id,
   314→                orchestrator_payload=orchestrator_payload,
   315→                parent_generation_id=parent_generation_id)
   316→        else:
   317→            orchestrator_logger.debug(f"[JOIN_ORCHESTRATOR] Using CHAIN pattern (legacy sequential)")
   318→            success, message = _create_join_chain_tasks(
   319→                clip_list=clip_list,
   320→                run_id=run_id,
   321→                join_settings=join_settings,
   322→                per_join_settings=per_join_settings,
   323→                vlm_enhanced_prompts=vlm_enhanced_prompts,
   324→                current_run_output_dir=current_run_output_dir,
   325→                orchestrator_task_id_str=orchestrator_task_id_str,
   326→                orchestrator_project_id=orchestrator_project_id,
   327→                orchestrator_payload=orchestrator_payload,
   328→                parent_generation_id=parent_generation_id)
   329→
   330→        orchestrator_logger.debug(f"[JOIN_ORCHESTRATOR] {message}")
   331→        return success, message
   332→
   333→    except (OSError, ValueError, RuntimeError, KeyError, TypeError) as e:
   334→        msg = f"Failed during join orchestration: {e}"
   335→        orchestrator_logger.debug(f"[JOIN_ORCHESTRATOR] ERROR: {msg}", exc_info=True)
   336→        return False, msg
   337→
   338→
   339→# Public alias for cross-module use.
   340→handle_join_clips_orchestrator_task = _handle_join_clips_orchestrator_task
   341→
```
