1→"""FFmpeg-based video operations: creation, FPS conversion, frame extraction to video."""
     2→import subprocess
     3→import threading
     4→from pathlib import Path
     5→
     6→import cv2
     7→import numpy as np
     8→
     9→from source.core.log import generation_logger
    10→from source.media.video.video_info import (
    11→    get_video_frame_count_and_fps,
    12→    get_video_frame_count_ffprobe,
    13→)
    14→
    15→__all__ = [
    16→    "ensure_video_fps",
    17→    "extract_frame_range_to_video",
    18→    "create_video_from_frames_list",
    19→    "apply_saturation_to_video_ffmpeg",
    20→    "video_has_audio",
    21→    "mux_audio_from_segments",
    22→]
    23→
    24→
    25→def ensure_video_fps(
    26→    input_video_path: str | Path,
    27→    target_fps: float,
    28→    output_dir: str | Path | None = None,
    29→    fps_tolerance: float = 0.5,
    30→) -> Path:
    31→    """
    32→    Ensure a video is at the target FPS, resampling if necessary.
    33→
    34→    This is important when frame indices are calculated for a specific FPS
    35→    (e.g., from frontend) but the actual video file may be at a different FPS.
    36→
    37→    Args:
    38→        input_video_path: Path to source video
    39→        target_fps: Desired FPS (e.g., 16)
    40→        output_dir: Directory for resampled video (defaults to same dir as source)
    41→        fps_tolerance: Maximum FPS difference before resampling (default 0.5)
    42→
    43→    Returns:
    44→        Path to video at target FPS (original if already correct, resampled if not)
    45→
    46→    Raises:
    47→        OSError: If the source video does not exist or the resampled output is missing/empty
    48→        RuntimeError: If FFmpeg fails or times out
    49→        ValueError: If the video FPS cannot be determined
    50→
    51→    Example:
    52→        # Ensure video is at 16fps before frame-based extraction
    53→        video_16fps = ensure_video_fps(downloaded_video, 16, work_dir)
    54→        extract_frame_range_to_video(video_16fps, output, 0, 252, 16)
    55→    """
    56→    input_video_path = Path(input_video_path)
    57→
    58→    # Validate target_fps is not None
    59→    if target_fps is None:
    60→        generation_logger.warning(f"[ENSURE_FPS] target_fps is None, defaulting to 16")
    61→        target_fps = 16
    62→
    63→    if not input_video_path.exists():
    64→        raise OSError(f"Video does not exist: {input_video_path}")
    65→
    66→    # Get actual fps
    67→    actual_frames, actual_fps = get_video_frame_count_and_fps(str(input_video_path))
    68→    if not actual_fps:
    69→        raise ValueError(f"Could not determine video FPS: {input_video_path}")
    70→
    71→    # Check if resampling is needed
    72→    if abs(actual_fps - target_fps) <= fps_tolerance:
    73→        generation_logger.debug(f"[ENSURE_FPS] Video already at target FPS: {actual_fps} fps (target: {target_fps}, tolerance: \u00b1{fps_tolerance})")
    74→        return input_video_path
    75→
    76→    # Need to resample
    77→    generation_logger.debug(f"[ENSURE_FPS] FPS mismatch: actual={actual_fps}, target={target_fps}")
    78→    generation_logger.debug(f"[ENSURE_FPS] Resampling {input_video_path.name} to {target_fps} fps...")
    79→
    80→    # Determine output path
    81→    if output_dir is None:
    82→        output_dir = input_video_path.parent
    83→    output_dir = Path(output_dir)
    84→    output_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    85→
    86→    resampled_path = output_dir / f"{input_video_path.stem}_resampled_{int(target_fps)}fps.mp4"
    87→
    88→    # Use fps filter (not -r output option) for accurate frame-based resampling
    89→    # The fps filter selects frames based on timestamps, ensuring frame N = time N/fps
    90→    # This is critical for frame-accurate extraction where frame indices must match timestamps
    91→    resample_cmd = [
    92→        'ffmpeg', '-y',
    93→        '-i', str(input_video_path),
    94→        '-vf', f'fps={target_fps}',
    95→        '-an',  # No audio for now
    96→        str(resampled_path)
    97→    ]
    98→
    99→    try:
   100→        result = subprocess.run(resample_cmd, capture_output=True, text=True, timeout=600)
   101→    except subprocess.TimeoutExpired as e:
   102→        raise RuntimeError(f"FFmpeg resample timed out after 600s for {input_video_path}") from e
   103→    except (subprocess.SubprocessError, OSError) as e:
   104→        raise RuntimeError(f"FFmpeg resample failed for {input_video_path}: {e}") from e
   105→
   106→    if result.returncode != 0:
   107→        raise RuntimeError(f"FFmpeg resample failed (rc={result.returncode}): {result.stderr[:500]}")
   108→
   109→    if not resampled_path.exists() or resampled_path.stat().st_size == 0:
   110→        raise OSError(f"Resampled video missing or empty: {resampled_path}")
   111→
   112→    # Verify result
   113→    new_frames, new_fps = get_video_frame_count_and_fps(str(resampled_path))
   114→    generation_logger.debug(f"[ENSURE_FPS] Resampled: {new_frames} frames @ {new_fps} fps")
   115→
   116→    return resampled_path
   117→
   118→
   119→def extract_frame_range_to_video(
   120→    input_video_path: str | Path,
   121→    output_video_path: str | Path,
   122→    start_frame: int,
   123→    end_frame: int | None,
   124→    fps: float,
   125→) -> Path:
   126→    """
   127→    Extract a range of frames from a video to a new video file using FFmpeg.
   128→
   129→    Uses FFmpeg's select filter with frame-accurate selection (not time-based).
   130→    This is the canonical function for frame extraction - use this instead of
   131→    inline FFmpeg commands.
   132→
   133→    Args:
   134→        input_video_path: Path to source video file
   135→        output_video_path: Path for output video file
   136→        start_frame: First frame to include (0-indexed, inclusive)
   137→        end_frame: Last frame to include (0-indexed, inclusive), or None for all remaining frames
   138→        fps: Output framerate
   139→
   140→    Returns:
   141→        Path to extracted video
   142→
   143→    Raises:
   144→        OSError: If source video does not exist or output is missing/empty
   145→        ValueError: If frame range is invalid or frame count cannot be determined
   146→        RuntimeError: If FFmpeg fails, times out, or output frame count is wrong
   147→
   148→    Examples:
   149→        # Extract frames 0-252 (253 frames)
   150→        extract_frame_range_to_video(src, out, 0, 252, 16)
   151→
   152→        # Extract frames from 13 onwards (skip first 13)
   153→        extract_frame_range_to_video(src, out, 13, None, 16)
   154→    """
   155→    input_video_path = Path(input_video_path)
   156→    output_video_path = Path(output_video_path)
   157→    output_video_path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
   158→
   159→    # Verify source exists
   160→    if not input_video_path.exists():
   161→        raise OSError(f"Source video does not exist: {input_video_path}")
   162→
   163→    # Get source properties - use ffprobe for accurate frame count
   164→    source_frames = get_video_frame_count_ffprobe(str(input_video_path))
   165→    _, source_fps = get_video_frame_count_and_fps(str(input_video_path))
   166→
   167→    if not source_frames:
   168→        # Fallback to OpenCV if ffprobe fails
   169→        source_frames, source_fps = get_video_frame_count_and_fps(str(input_video_path))
   170→        generation_logger.warning(f"[EXTRACT_RANGE] Using OpenCV frame count (ffprobe failed): {source_frames}")
   171→
   172→    if not source_frames:
   173→        raise ValueError(f"Could not determine source video frame count: {input_video_path}")
   174→
   175→    # Validate range
   176→    if start_frame < 0:
   177→        raise ValueError(f"start_frame cannot be negative: {start_frame}")
   178→
   179→    if end_frame is not None and end_frame >= source_frames:
   180→        raise ValueError(f"end_frame {end_frame} >= source frames {source_frames}")
   181→
   182→    # Build filter based on whether we have an end_frame
   183→    if end_frame is not None:
   184→        # Extract specific range: frames start_frame to end_frame (inclusive)
   185→        filter_str = f"select=between(n\\,{start_frame}\\,{end_frame}),setpts=N/FR/TB"
   186→        expected_frames = end_frame - start_frame + 1
   187→        range_desc = f"frames {start_frame}-{end_frame} ({expected_frames} frames)"
   188→    else:
   189→        # Extract from start_frame onwards (skip first start_frame frames)
   190→        filter_str = f"select=gte(n\\,{start_frame}),setpts=N/FR/TB"
   191→        expected_frames = source_frames - start_frame
   192→        range_desc = f"frames {start_frame}-{source_frames-1} ({expected_frames} frames)"
   193→
   194→    generation_logger.debug(f"[EXTRACT_RANGE] Source: {input_video_path.name} ({source_frames} frames @ {source_fps} fps)")
   195→    generation_logger.debug(f"[EXTRACT_RANGE] Extracting: {range_desc}")
   196→
   197→    cmd = [
   198→        'ffmpeg', '-y',
   199→        '-i', str(input_video_path),
   200→        '-vf', filter_str,
   201→        '-c:v', 'libx264',
   202→        '-pix_fmt', 'yuv420p',
   203→        '-preset', 'slow',  # Better quality at same bitrate
   204→        '-crf', '10',  # Near-lossless quality for intermediate files
   205→        '-r', str(fps),
   206→        '-an',  # No audio
   207→        str(output_video_path)
   208→    ]
   209→
   210→    try:
   211→        result = subprocess.run(cmd, capture_output=True, text=True, timeout=300)
   212→    except subprocess.TimeoutExpired as e:
   213→        raise RuntimeError(f"FFmpeg frame extraction timed out after 300s for {input_video_path}") from e
   214→    except (subprocess.SubprocessError, OSError) as e:
   215→        raise RuntimeError(f"FFmpeg frame extraction failed for {input_video_path}: {e}") from e
   216→
   217→    if result.returncode != 0:
   218→        raise RuntimeError(f"FFmpeg frame extraction failed (rc={result.returncode}): {result.stderr[:500]}")
   219→
   220→    if not output_video_path.exists() or output_video_path.stat().st_size == 0:
   221→        raise OSError(f"Output video missing or empty: {output_video_path}")
   222→
   223→    # Verify frame count (allow small tolerance for OpenCV/FFmpeg discrepancies)
   224→    actual_frames, _ = get_video_frame_count_and_fps(str(output_video_path))
   225→    if actual_frames is None:
   226→        raise RuntimeError(f"Could not verify output frame count for {output_video_path}")
   227→
   228→    # Allow up to 3 frames difference - OpenCV and FFmpeg often disagree on frame counts
   229→    # especially for end-of-video segments or variable framerate videos
   230→    frame_diff = abs(actual_frames - expected_frames)
   231→    if frame_diff > 3:
   232→        raise RuntimeError(f"Frame count mismatch too large: expected {expected_frames}, got {actual_frames} (diff: {frame_diff})")
   233→    elif frame_diff > 0:
   234→        generation_logger.warning(f"[EXTRACT_RANGE] Minor frame count difference: expected {expected_frames}, got {actual_frames} (diff: {frame_diff}, within tolerance)")
   235→
   236→    generation_logger.debug(f"[EXTRACT_RANGE] Extracted {actual_frames} frames to {output_video_path.name}")
   237→    return output_video_path
   238→
   239→
   240→def create_video_from_frames_list(
   241→    frames_list: list[np.ndarray],
   242→    output_path: str | Path,
   243→    fps: int,
   244→    resolution: tuple[int, int],
   245→    *,
   246→    standardize_colorspace: bool = False
   247→) -> Path:
   248→    """Creates a video from a list of NumPy BGR frames using FFmpeg subprocess.
   249→
   250→    Uses streaming to pipe frames to FFmpeg incrementally, avoiding loading
   251→    all frame data into memory at once. This is critical for large videos
   252→    (e.g., 2000+ frames at 1080p would otherwise require 30+ GB RAM).
   253→
   254→    Args:
   255→        frames_list: List of BGR numpy arrays
   256→        output_path: Output video path
   257→        fps: Frames per second
   258→        resolution: (width, height) tuple
   259→        standardize_colorspace: If True, adds BT.709 colorspace standardization
   260→
   261→    Returns:
   262→        Path object of the successfully written file
   263→
   264→    Raises:
   265→        ValueError: If no valid frames are provided
   266→        RuntimeError: If FFmpeg fails, times out, or output is missing/empty
   267→        OSError: If FFmpeg is not installed or file system errors occur
   268→    """
   269→
   270→    output_path_obj = Path(output_path)
   271→    output_path_mp4 = output_path_obj.with_suffix('.mp4')
   272→    output_path_mp4.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
   273→
   274→    generation_logger.debug(f"[CREATE_VIDEO] Creating video: {output_path_mp4}")
   275→    generation_logger.debug(f"[CREATE_VIDEO] Input: {len(frames_list)} frames, resolution={resolution}, fps={fps}")
   276→
   277→    ffmpeg_cmd = [
   278→        "ffmpeg", "-y",
   279→        "-loglevel", "error",
   280→        "-f", "rawvideo",
   281→        "-vcodec", "rawvideo",
   282→        "-pix_fmt", "bgr24",
   283→        "-s", f"{resolution[0]}x{resolution[1]}",
   284→        "-r", str(fps),
   285→        "-i", "-",
   286→        "-c:v", "libx264",
   287→        "-pix_fmt", "yuv420p",
   288→        "-preset", "slow",  # Better quality at same bitrate
   289→        "-crf", "10",  # Near-lossless quality for intermediate files
   290→    ]
   291→
   292→    # Add colorspace standardization if requested
   293→    if standardize_colorspace:
   294→        ffmpeg_cmd.extend([
   295→            "-vf", "format=yuv420p,colorspace=bt709:iall=bt709:fast=1",
   296→            "-color_primaries", "bt709",
   297→            "-color_trc", "bt709",
   298→            "-colorspace", "bt709",
   299→        ])
   300→
   301→    ffmpeg_cmd.append(str(output_path_mp4.resolve()))
   302→
   303→    # Count valid frames first (without storing them)
   304→    valid_count = 0
   305→    skipped_none = 0
   306→    skipped_invalid = 0
   307→    for frame_np in frames_list:
   308→        if frame_np is None or not isinstance(frame_np, np.ndarray):
   309→            skipped_none += 1
   310→        elif len(frame_np.shape) != 3 or frame_np.shape[2] != 3:
   311→            skipped_invalid += 1
   312→        else:
   313→            valid_count += 1
   314→
   315→    if skipped_none > 0:
   316→        generation_logger.warning(f"[CREATE_VIDEO] Will skip {skipped_none} None/invalid frames")
   317→    if skipped_invalid > 0:
   318→        generation_logger.warning(f"[CREATE_VIDEO] Will skip {skipped_invalid} non-RGB frames")
   319→
   320→    if valid_count == 0:
   321→        raise ValueError(f"No valid frames to process! Input had {len(frames_list)} frames, all invalid")
   322→
   323→    generation_logger.debug(f"[CREATE_VIDEO] Streaming {valid_count} frames to FFmpeg (memory-efficient mode)")
   324→
   325→    try:
   326→        # Use Popen to stream frames incrementally instead of loading all into memory
   327→        proc = subprocess.Popen(
   328→            ffmpeg_cmd,
   329→            stdin=subprocess.PIPE,
   330→            stdout=subprocess.PIPE,
   331→            stderr=subprocess.PIPE
   332→        )
   333→    except FileNotFoundError as e:
   334→        raise OSError("FFmpeg not found - is it installed?") from e
   335→    except (subprocess.SubprocessError, OSError) as e:
   336→        raise RuntimeError(f"Failed to start FFmpeg process: {e}") from e
   337→
   338→    frames_written = 0
   339→    resize_warnings = 0
   340→
   341→    for frame_idx, frame_np in enumerate(frames_list):
   342→        # Skip invalid frames
   343→        if frame_np is None or not isinstance(frame_np, np.ndarray):
   344→            continue
   345→        if len(frame_np.shape) != 3 or frame_np.shape[2] != 3:
   346→            continue
   347→
   348→        # Ensure uint8
   349→        if frame_np.dtype != np.uint8:
   350→            frame_np = frame_np.astype(np.uint8)
   351→
   352→        # Resize if needed
   353→        if frame_np.shape[0] != resolution[1] or frame_np.shape[1] != resolution[0]:
   354→            try:
   355→                frame_np = cv2.resize(frame_np, resolution, interpolation=cv2.INTER_AREA)
   356→            except (OSError, ValueError, RuntimeError) as e:
   357→                if resize_warnings < 5:
   358→                    generation_logger.warning(f"[CREATE_VIDEO] Failed to resize frame {frame_idx}: {e}")
   359→                resize_warnings += 1
   360→                continue
   361→
   362→        # Write frame bytes directly to FFmpeg stdin
   363→        try:
   364→            proc.stdin.write(frame_np.tobytes())
   365→            frames_written += 1
   366→        except BrokenPipeError:
   367→            generation_logger.error(f"[CREATE_VIDEO] FFmpeg pipe broken after {frames_written} frames")
   368→            break
   369→
   370→    if resize_warnings > 5:
   371→        generation_logger.warning(f"[CREATE_VIDEO] {resize_warnings} total resize failures (only first 5 logged)")
   372→
   373→    # Close stdin to signal end of input
   374→    proc.stdin.close()
   375→
   376→    generation_logger.debug(f"[CREATE_VIDEO] Wrote {frames_written} frames to FFmpeg, waiting for encoding...")
   377→
   378→    # Read stderr in background thread to avoid blocking
   379→    stderr_output = []
   380→    def read_stderr():
   381→        try:
   382→            stderr_output.append(proc.stderr.read())
   383→        except OSError as e:
   384→            generation_logger.debug(f"[CREATE_VIDEO] Failed to read FFmpeg stderr: {e}")
   385→
   386→    stderr_thread = threading.Thread(target=read_stderr)
   387→    stderr_thread.start()
   388→
   389→    try:
   390→        proc.wait(timeout=300)  # 5 minute timeout for encoding
   391→    except subprocess.TimeoutExpired:
   392→        proc.kill()
   393→        raise RuntimeError(f"FFmpeg timed out after 300 seconds creating video {output_path_mp4}")
   394→    finally:
   395→        stderr_thread.join(timeout=5)
   396→
   397→    stderr = stderr_output[0] if stderr_output else b""
   398→
   399→    if proc.returncode == 0:
   400→        if output_path_mp4.exists() and output_path_mp4.stat().st_size > 0:
   401→            generation_logger.debug(f"[CREATE_VIDEO] SUCCESS: Created {output_path_mp4} ({output_path_mp4.stat().st_size} bytes)")
   402→            return output_path_mp4
   403→        raise RuntimeError(f"FFmpeg succeeded but output file missing or empty: {output_path_mp4}")
   404→    else:
   405→        stderr_str = stderr.decode('utf-8', errors='replace') if stderr else "no stderr"
   406→        if output_path_mp4.exists():
   407→            try:
   408→                output_path_mp4.unlink()
   409→            except OSError as e:
   410→                generation_logger.debug(f"[CREATE_VIDEO] Failed to clean up partial output file {output_path_mp4}: {e}")
   411→        raise RuntimeError(f"FFmpeg failed (rc={proc.returncode}): {stderr_str[:500]}")
   412→
   413→
   414→def apply_saturation_to_video_ffmpeg(
   415→    input_video_path: str | Path,
   416→    output_video_path: str | Path,
   417→    saturation_level: float,
   418→    preset: str = "medium"
   419→) -> bool:
   420→    """Applies a saturation adjustment to the full video using FFmpeg's eq filter.
   421→    Returns: True if FFmpeg succeeds and the output file exists & is non-empty, else False.
   422→    """
   423→    inp = Path(input_video_path)
   424→    outp = Path(output_video_path)
   425→    outp.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
   426→
   427→    cmd = [
   428→        "ffmpeg", "-y",
   429→        "-i", str(inp.resolve()),
   430→        "-vf", f"eq=saturation={saturation_level}",
   431→        "-c:v", "libx264",
   432→        "-crf", "10",  # Near-lossless quality for intermediate files
   433→        "-preset", preset,
   434→        "-pix_fmt", "yuv420p",
   435→        "-an",
   436→        str(outp.resolve())
   437→    ]
   438→
   439→    try:
   440→        subprocess.run(cmd, check=True, capture_output=True, text=True, encoding="utf-8", timeout=600)
   441→        if outp.exists() and outp.stat().st_size > 0:
   442→            return True
   443→        return False
   444→    except subprocess.CalledProcessError:
   445→        return False
   446→
   447→
   448→def video_has_audio(video_path: str | Path) -> bool:
   449→    """Check if a video file contains an audio stream."""
   450→    try:
   451→        result = subprocess.run(
   452→            ["ffprobe", "-v", "quiet", "-select_streams", "a",
   453→             "-show_entries", "stream=codec_type", "-of", "csv=p=0",
   454→             str(video_path)],
   455→            capture_output=True, text=True, timeout=10,
   456→        )
   457→        return "audio" in result.stdout
   458→    except (subprocess.SubprocessError, OSError):
   459→        return False
   460→
   461→
   462→def mux_audio_from_segments(
   463→    silent_video: str | Path,
   464→    segment_videos: list[str | Path],
   465→    output_path: str | Path,
   466→    overlap_frames: list[int] | None = None,
   467→    fps: float = 24.0,
   468→) -> Path | None:
   469→    """Concatenate audio from segment videos and mux onto a silent stitched video.
   470→
   471→    Extracts audio from each segment, trims overlap regions to match the video
   472→    stitch, concatenates, and muxes the result onto *silent_video*.
   473→
   474→    Args:
   475→        silent_video: Path to the stitched video (no audio).
   476→        segment_videos: Ordered list of segment video paths (with audio).
   477→        output_path: Destination path for the video+audio result.
   478→        overlap_frames: Frame overlaps between adjacent segments (len = n-1).
   479→        fps: Frames per second (used to convert overlap frames to seconds).
   480→
   481→    Returns:
   482→        Path to the output video with audio, or None on failure.
   483→    """
   484→    silent_video = Path(silent_video)
   485→    output_path = Path(output_path)
   486→
   487→    # Quick check: do any segments have audio?
   488→    segments_with_audio = [p for p in segment_videos if video_has_audio(p)]
   489→    if not segments_with_audio:
   490→        generation_logger.debug("[AUDIO_MUX] No segment videos contain audio — skipping audio mux")
   491→        return None
   492→
   493→    generation_logger.debug(
   494→        f"[AUDIO_MUX] {len(segments_with_audio)}/{len(segment_videos)} segments have audio, "
   495→        f"muxing onto stitched video"
   496→    )
   497→
   498→    try:
   499→        import tempfile
   500→
   501→        work_dir = Path(tempfile.mkdtemp(prefix="audio_mux_"))
   502→        overlaps = overlap_frames or [0] * (len(segment_videos) - 1)
   503→
   504→        # --- Step 1: Extract audio from each segment, trimming overlaps ---
   505→        audio_parts: list[Path] = []
   506→        for i, seg_path in enumerate(segment_videos):
   507→            seg_path = Path(seg_path)
   508→            if not video_has_audio(seg_path):
   509→                # Generate silence matching this segment's duration
   510→                try:
   511→                    dur_result = subprocess.run(
   512→                        ["ffprobe", "-v", "quiet", "-show_entries", "format=duration",
   513→                         "-of", "csv=p=0", str(seg_path)],
   514→                        capture_output=True, text=True, timeout=10,
   515→                    )
   516→                    duration = float(dur_result.stdout.strip())
   517→                except (subprocess.SubprocessError, OSError, ValueError):
   518→                    duration = 1.0
   519→
   520→                # Trim overlap from start (except first segment)
   521→                if i > 0 and i - 1 < len(overlaps):
   522→                    trim_start = overlaps[i - 1] / fps
   523→                    duration = max(0.01, duration - trim_start)
   524→
   525→                silence_path = work_dir / f"silence_{i}.aac"
   526→                subprocess.run(
   527→                    ["ffmpeg", "-y", "-f", "lavfi", "-i",
   528→                     f"anullsrc=channel_layout=stereo:sample_rate=44100",
   529→                     "-t", f"{duration:.4f}", "-c:a", "aac", str(silence_path)],
   530→                    capture_output=True, timeout=30,
   531→                )
   532→                if silence_path.exists():
   533→                    audio_parts.append(silence_path)
   534→                continue
   535→
   536→            part_path = work_dir / f"audio_{i}.aac"
   537→
   538→            # Build ffmpeg command with optional trim
   539→            cmd = ["ffmpeg", "-y", "-i", str(seg_path), "-vn", "-c:a", "aac"]
   540→
   541→            if i > 0 and i - 1 < len(overlaps) and overlaps[i - 1] > 0:
   542→                # Trim overlap seconds from the beginning of this segment's audio
   543→                trim_seconds = overlaps[i - 1] / fps
   544→                cmd.extend(["-ss", f"{trim_seconds:.4f}"])
   545→
   546→            cmd.append(str(part_path))
   547→            subprocess.run(cmd, capture_output=True, timeout=120)
   548→
   549→            if part_path.exists() and part_path.stat().st_size > 0:
   550→                audio_parts.append(part_path)
   551→
   552→        if not audio_parts:
   553→            generation_logger.debug("[AUDIO_MUX] No audio parts extracted — skipping")
   554→            return None
   555→
   556→        # --- Step 2: Concatenate audio parts ---
   557→        concat_list = work_dir / "concat.txt"
   558→        concat_list.write_text(
   559→            "\n".join(f"file '{p.resolve()}'" for p in audio_parts),
   560→            encoding="utf-8",
   561→        )
   562→
   563→        merged_audio = work_dir / "merged_audio.aac"
   564→        subprocess.run(
   565→            ["ffmpeg", "-y", "-f", "concat", "-safe", "0",
   566→             "-i", str(concat_list), "-c:a", "aac", str(merged_audio)],
   567→            capture_output=True, timeout=120,
   568→        )
   569→
   570→        if not merged_audio.exists() or merged_audio.stat().st_size == 0:
   571→            generation_logger.warning("[AUDIO_MUX] Audio concatenation failed")
   572→            return None
   573→
   574→        # --- Step 3: Mux merged audio onto the silent video ---
   575→        # Use -shortest so the output matches the video duration
   576→        subprocess.run(
   577→            ["ffmpeg", "-y",
   578→             "-i", str(silent_video),
   579→             "-i", str(merged_audio),
   580→             "-c:v", "copy", "-c:a", "aac", "-shortest",
   581→             str(output_path)],
   582→            capture_output=True, timeout=120,
   583→        )
   584→
   585→        if output_path.exists() and output_path.stat().st_size > 0:
   586→            generation_logger.debug(f"[AUDIO_MUX] Successfully muxed audio → {output_path}")
   587→            # Clean up temp dir
   588→            import shutil
   589→            shutil.rmtree(work_dir, ignore_errors=True)
   590→            return output_path
   591→
   592→        generation_logger.warning("[AUDIO_MUX] Final mux produced empty output")
   593→        return None
   594→
   595→    except (subprocess.SubprocessError, OSError, ValueError) as e:
   596→        generation_logger.warning(f"[AUDIO_MUX] Audio mux failed: {e}")
   597→        return None
   598→
```

1→"""Generation strategy convenience methods for WanOrchestrator.
     2→
     3→Provides typed wrappers (T2V, VACE, Flux, config-based) around the core
     4→:meth:`WanOrchestrator.generate` method.  Each function is bound to the
     5→orchestrator at class-definition time via ``orchestrator.py``.
     6→"""
     7→
     8→from typing import TYPE_CHECKING, Optional
     9→
    10→from source.core.log import generation_logger
    11→
    12→if TYPE_CHECKING:
    13→    from source.core.params import TaskConfig
    14→
    15→
    16→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    17→# Text-to-Video
    18→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    19→
    20→def generate_t2v(self, prompt: str, model_type: str = None, **kwargs) -> str:
    21→    """Generate text-to-video content.
    22→
    23→    Thin wrapper around :meth:`WanOrchestrator.generate` that adds a
    24→    warning if the currently-loaded model is not a T2V model.
    25→
    26→    Args:
    27→        self: WanOrchestrator instance.
    28→        prompt: Text prompt for generation.
    29→        model_type: Optional model override.
    30→        **kwargs: Additional parameters forwarded to generate().
    31→
    32→    Returns:
    33→        Path to generated video file.
    34→    """
    35→    if not self._is_t2v():
    36→        generation_logger.warning(f"Current model {self.current_model} may not be optimized for T2V")
    37→    return self.generate(prompt=prompt, model_type=model_type, **kwargs)
    38→
    39→
    40→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    41→# VACE (Video Any Controllable Edit)
    42→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    43→
    44→def generate_vace(
    45→    self,
    46→    prompt: str,
    47→    video_guide: str,
    48→    model_type: str = None,
    49→    video_mask: Optional[str] = None,
    50→    video_prompt_type: str = "VP",
    51→    control_net_weight: float = 1.0,
    52→    control_net_weight2: float = 1.0,
    53→    **kwargs,
    54→) -> str:
    55→    """Generate VACE controlled video content.
    56→
    57→    Thin wrapper around :meth:`WanOrchestrator.generate` that adds
    58→    a warning if the currently-loaded model is not a VACE model.
    59→
    60→    Args:
    61→        self: WanOrchestrator instance.
    62→        prompt: Text prompt for generation.
    63→        video_guide: Path to primary control video (required).
    64→        model_type: Optional model override.
    65→        video_mask: Path to primary mask video (optional).
    66→        video_prompt_type: VACE encoding type (e.g., "VP", "VPD", "VPDA").
    67→        control_net_weight: Strength for first VACE encoding.
    68→        control_net_weight2: Strength for second VACE encoding.
    69→        **kwargs: Additional parameters forwarded to generate().
    70→
    71→    Returns:
    72→        Path to generated video file.
    73→    """
    74→    if not self._is_vace():
    75→        generation_logger.warning(f"Current model {self.current_model} may not be a VACE model")
    76→
    77→    # Log LoRA parameters at VACE level
    78→    generation_logger.debug(f"generate_vace received kwargs: {list(kwargs.keys())}")
    79→    if "lora_names" in kwargs:
    80→        generation_logger.debug(f"generate_vace lora_names: {kwargs['lora_names']}")
    81→    if "lora_multipliers" in kwargs:
    82→        generation_logger.debug(f"generate_vace lora_multipliers: {kwargs['lora_multipliers']}")
    83→
    84→    return self.generate(
    85→        prompt=prompt,
    86→        model_type=model_type,
    87→        video_guide=video_guide,
    88→        video_mask=video_mask,
    89→        video_prompt_type=video_prompt_type,
    90→        control_net_weight=control_net_weight,
    91→        control_net_weight2=control_net_weight2,
    92→        **kwargs,
    93→    )
    94→
    95→
    96→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    97→# Flux image generation
    98→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    99→
   100→def generate_flux(self, prompt: str, images: int = 4, model_type: str = None, **kwargs) -> str:
   101→    """Generate Flux images.
   102→
   103→    Thin wrapper around :meth:`WanOrchestrator.generate` that adds a
   104→    warning if the currently-loaded model is not a Flux model.
   105→
   106→    Args:
   107→        self: WanOrchestrator instance.
   108→        prompt: Text prompt for generation.
   109→        images: Number of images to generate (maps to video_length).
   110→        model_type: Optional model override.
   111→        **kwargs: Additional parameters forwarded to generate().
   112→
   113→    Returns:
   114→        Path to generated image(s).
   115→    """
   116→    if not self._is_flux():
   117→        generation_logger.warning(f"Current model {self.current_model} may not be a Flux model")
   118→
   119→    return self.generate(
   120→        prompt=prompt,
   121→        model_type=model_type,
   122→        video_length=images,  # For Flux, video_length = number of images
   123→        **kwargs,
   124→    )
   125→
   126→
   127→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   128→# Config-based generation (Qwen / universal typed API)
   129→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   130→
   131→def generate_with_config(self, config: 'TaskConfig') -> str:
   132→    """Generate content using a typed TaskConfig object.
   133→
   134→    This is the new typed API that provides cleaner parameter handling:
   135→    1. All parameters are already parsed and validated in TaskConfig
   136→    2. LoRAs are already resolved (downloaded if needed)
   137→    3. Phase config is already applied
   138→
   139→    Works for all model types (T2V, VACE, Flux, Qwen) but is the
   140→    primary entry-point for Qwen-style image generation.
   141→
   142→    Args:
   143→        self: WanOrchestrator instance.
   144→        config: TaskConfig object with all generation parameters.
   145→
   146→    Returns:
   147→        Path to generated output file(s).
   148→
   149→    Raises:
   150→        TypeError: If config is not a TaskConfig instance.
   151→        RuntimeError: If no model is loaded.
   152→        ValueError: If no prompt is provided.
   153→
   154→    Example::
   155→
   156→        from source.core.params import TaskConfig
   157→        config = TaskConfig.from_db_task(task.parameters, task_id=task.id)
   158→        result = orchestrator.generate_with_config(config)
   159→    """
   160→    # Import here to avoid circular import
   161→    from source.core.params import TaskConfig
   162→
   163→    if not isinstance(config, TaskConfig):
   164→        raise TypeError(f"Expected TaskConfig, got {type(config)}")
   165→
   166→    if not self.current_model:
   167→        raise RuntimeError("No model loaded. Call load_model() first.")
   168→
   169→    # Log what we're generating
   170→    generation_logger.info(f"[GENERATE_CONFIG] Task {config.task_id}: Starting generation with typed config")
   171→    if not config.lora.is_empty():
   172→        generation_logger.info(f"[GENERATE_CONFIG] Task {config.task_id}: LoRAs: {config.lora.filenames}")
   173→    if not config.phase.is_empty():
   174→        generation_logger.info(f"[GENERATE_CONFIG] Task {config.task_id}: Phases: {config.phase.num_phases}")
   175→
   176→    # Convert to WGP format
   177→    wgp_params = config.to_wgp_format()
   178→
   179→    # Ensure prompt is set
   180→    prompt = wgp_params.pop('prompt', config.generation.prompt)
   181→    if not prompt:
   182→        raise ValueError("No prompt provided in TaskConfig")
   183→
   184→    # Determine model type
   185→    model_type = wgp_params.pop('model', None) or config.model or self.current_model
   186→
   187→    # Call the standard generate method with unpacked params
   188→    return self.generate(
   189→        prompt=prompt,
   190→        model_type=model_type,
   191→        **wgp_params,
   192→    )
   193→
```

1→"""
     2→WGP Parameter Resolution
     3→
     4→Resolves generation parameters with explicit precedence:
     5→1. Task explicit parameters (highest priority)
     6→2. Model JSON configuration (medium priority)
     7→3. System defaults (lowest priority)
     8→"""
     9→
    10→from source.core.log import (

    13→)

    15→
    16→def resolve_parameters(orchestrator, model_type: str, task_params: dict) -> dict:
    17→    """
    18→    Resolve generation parameters with explicit precedence:
    19→    1. Task explicit parameters (highest priority)
    20→    2. Model JSON configuration (medium priority)
    21→    3. System defaults (lowest priority)
    22→
    23→    Args:
    24→        orchestrator: WanOrchestrator instance (used for logger access; currently

    27→        task_params: Parameters explicitly provided by the task/user
    28→
    29→    Returns:
    30→        Resolved parameter dictionary with proper precedence
    31→    """
    32→    # 1. Start with system defaults (lowest priority) - migrated from worker.py
    33→    resolved_params = {
    34→        "resolution": "1280x720",
    35→        "video_length": 49,

    43→        "negative_prompt": "",
    44→        "activated_loras": [],
    45→        "loras_multipliers": "",
    46→    }
    47→
    48→    # 2. Apply model JSON configuration (medium priority)
    49→    try:
    50→        import wgp
    51→        model_defaults = wgp.get_default_settings(model_type)
    52→        # Safe logging: Use safe_dict_repr to prevent hanging
    53→        generation_logger.debug(f"get_default_settings('{model_type}') returned: {safe_dict_repr(model_defaults) if model_defaults else 'None'}")
    54→        generation_logger.debug(f"Type: {type(model_defaults)}")
    55→
    56→        if model_defaults:
    57→            # DIAGNOSTIC: Log model_defaults structure before iteration
    58→            generation_logger.info(f"🔍 DIAGNOSTIC: model_defaults has {len(model_defaults)} parameters")
    59→            generation_logger.info(f"🔍 DIAGNOSTIC: model_defaults keys: {list(model_defaults.keys())}")
    60→            generation_logger.info(f"🔍 DIAGNOSTIC: model_defaults is type: {type(model_defaults)}")
    61→            generation_logger.info(f"🔍 DIAGNOSTIC: model_defaults id: {id(model_defaults)}")
    62→
    63→            # AGGRESSIVE: Dump full model_defaults values

    65→            for key in list(model_defaults.keys())[:10]:  # First 10 for safety
    66→                val = model_defaults.get(key)
    67→                generation_logger.info(f"  {key}: {type(val).__name__} = {repr(val)[:200]}")
    68→            generation_logger.info(f"  ... and {max(0, len(model_defaults) - 10)} more")
    69→
    70→            # Safe logging: Only show keys before applying model config
    71→            generation_logger.debug(f"Before applying model config - resolved_params keys: {list(resolved_params.keys())}")
    72→
    73→            # DEFENSIVE: Create a snapshot of items to prevent iterator invalidation

    76→

    80→
    81→                # JSON passthrough mode: Allow activated_loras and loras_multipliers to pass directly

    89→                        if resolved_params is not None:

    92→                    except Exception as diag_e:

   100→                            guidance_keys = [k for k in resolved_params.keys() if 'guidance' in k or 'switch' in k or 'phase' in k]
   101→                            for k in guidance_keys:

   104→                        else:
   105→                            generation_logger.info(f"  WARNING: resolved_params is {type(resolved_params)}, not dict!")
   106→
   107→                    try:

   109→                    except Exception as e:
   110→                        # Catch the exact error and log full context
   111→                        generation_logger.critical(f"[CRASH_POINT] LOOP [{idx+1}] failed at resolved_params.get('{param}')")
   112→                        generation_logger.critical(f"[CRASH_POINT] Error type: {type(e).__name__}: {e}")
   113→                        generation_logger.critical(f"[CRASH_POINT] resolved_params is: {resolved_params}")
   114→                        generation_logger.critical(f"[CRASH_POINT] resolved_params type: {type(resolved_params)}")
   115→                        import traceback
   116→                        generation_logger.critical(f"[CRASH_POINT] Full traceback:\n{traceback.format_exc()}")
   117→                        raise
   118→
   119→                    # DEFENSIVE: Skip None values from model defaults (they shouldn't override)
   120→                    if value is None:
   121→                        generation_logger.debug(f"⏭️  LOOP [{idx+1}]: Skipped '{param}' - model default is None (invalid)")
   122→                        continue
   123→
   124→                    generation_logger.debug(f"🔍 LOOP [{idx+1}]: Assigning new value for '{param}'")
   125→                    resolved_params[param] = value
   126→
   127→                    # AGGRESSIVE: Log after assignment
   128→                    if param in ['guidance_phases', 'guidance_scale', 'guidance2_scale', 'guidance3_scale']:
   129→                        generation_logger.info(f"[AGGRESSIVE] LOOP [{idx+1}] POST-ASSIGNMENT:")
   130→                        generation_logger.info(f"  resolved_params['{param}'] = {repr(resolved_params.get(param))[:100]}")
   131→                        generation_logger.info(f"  old_value was: {repr(old_value)[:100]}")
   132→                        generation_logger.info(f"  value param was: {repr(value)[:100]}")
   133→
   134→                    generation_logger.debug(f"🔍 LOOP [{idx+1}]: Logging change for '{param}'")
   135→                    # Safe logging: Use safe_log_change to prevent hanging on large values
   136→                    try:
   137→                        change_log = safe_log_change(param, old_value, value)
   138→                        generation_logger.debug(change_log)
   139→                    except Exception as log_e:
   140→                        # Catch logging errors and log them separately
   141→                        generation_logger.critical(f"[CRASH_POINT] LOOP [{idx+1}] failed at safe_log_change('{param}')")
   142→                        generation_logger.critical(f"[CRASH_POINT] Error type: {type(log_e).__name__}: {log_e}")
   143→                        generation_logger.critical(f"[CRASH_POINT] old_value: {old_value}, type: {type(old_value)}")
   144→                        generation_logger.critical(f"[CRASH_POINT] value: {value}, type: {type(value)}")
   145→                        import traceback
   146→                        generation_logger.critical(f"[CRASH_POINT] Full traceback:\n{traceback.format_exc()}")
   147→                        raise
   148→
   149→                    generation_logger.debug(f"✅ LOOP [{idx+1}]: Completed '{param}'")
   150→                else:
   151→                    generation_logger.info(f"⏭️  LOOP [{idx+1}]: Skipped '{param}' (excluded)")
   152→
   153→            generation_logger.info(f"✅ DIAGNOSTIC: Loop completed successfully, processed {len(model_items)} items")
   154→
   155→            # Safe logging: Only show keys after applying model config
   156→            generation_logger.debug(f"After applying model config - resolved_params keys: {list(resolved_params.keys())}")
   157→            generation_logger.debug(f"Applied model config for '{model_type}': {len(model_defaults)} parameters")
   158→        else:
   159→            generation_logger.warning(f"No model configuration found for '{model_type}'")
   160→
   161→    except (ValueError, KeyError, TypeError) as e:
   162→        generation_logger.warning(f"Could not load model configuration for '{model_type}': {e}")
   163→        generation_logger.debug(f"Exception details: {str(e)}")
   164→        import traceback
   165→        generation_logger.debug(f"Traceback: {traceback.format_exc()}")
   166→
   167→    # 3. Apply task explicit parameters (highest priority)
   168→    # Safe logging: Use safe_dict_repr to prevent hanging on large nested structures
   169→    generation_logger.debug(safe_log_params(task_params, "Task explicit parameters"))
   170→    generation_logger.debug(f"Before applying task params - resolved_params keys: {list(resolved_params.keys())}")
   171→
   172→    for param, value in task_params.items():
   173→        if value is not None:  # Don't override with None values
   174→            old_value = resolved_params.get(param, "NOT_SET")
   175→            resolved_params[param] = value
   176→            # Avoid logging large nested dicts that can hang
   177→            if param in ["orchestrator_payload", "orchestrator_details", "full_orchestrator_payload"]:
   178→                generation_logger.debug(f"Task override {param}: <large dict with {len(value) if isinstance(value, dict) else '?'} keys>")
   179→            else:
   180→                # Limit string representation to prevent hanging on large values
   181→                try:
   182→                    old_str = str(old_value)
   183→                    value_str = str(value)
   184→                    max_chars = 500
   185→                    if len(old_str) > max_chars:
   186→                        old_str = old_str[:max_chars] + "..."
   187→                    if len(value_str) > max_chars:
   188→                        value_str = value_str[:max_chars] + "..."
   189→                    generation_logger.debug(f"Task override {param}: {old_str} → {value_str}")
   190→                except (ValueError, KeyError, TypeError) as e:

   192→
   193→    # Log keys only to avoid hanging on large nested structures
   194→    generation_logger.debug(f"FINAL resolved_params keys: {list(resolved_params.keys())}")
   195→    generation_logger.debug(f"Parameter resolution for '{model_type}': {len(task_params)} task overrides applied")
   196→    return resolved_params

1→"""
     2→Join clips generation handler - bridge two video clips using VACE generation.
     3→
     4→This module provides the main handle_join_clips_task function that:
     5→1. Optionally standardizes both videos to a target aspect ratio (via center-crop)
     6→2. Extracts context frames from the end of the first clip
     7→3. Extracts context frames from the beginning of the second clip
     8→4. Generates transition frames between them using VACE
     9→5. Uses mask video to preserve the context frames and only generate the gap
    10→"""
    11→
    12→import json
    13→import time
    14→from pathlib import Path
    15→from typing import Tuple
    16→
    17→# Import shared utilities
    18→from ...utils import (
    19→    ensure_valid_prompt,
    20→    ensure_valid_negative_prompt,
    21→    get_video_frame_count_and_fps,
    22→    download_video_if_url,
    23→    save_frame_from_video,
    24→    prepare_output_path_with_upload,
    25→    upload_and_get_final_output_location,
    26→    upload_intermediate_file_to_storage
    27→)
    28→from ...media.video import (
    29→    extract_frames_from_video,
    30→    extract_frame_range_to_video,
    31→    ensure_video_fps,
    32→    standardize_video_aspect_ratio,
    33→    stitch_videos_with_crossfade,
    34→    create_video_from_frames_list,
    35→    get_video_frame_count_ffprobe,
    36→    get_video_fps_ffprobe,
    37→    add_audio_to_video)
    38→from source.media.video.vace_frame_utils import (
    39→    create_guide_and_mask_for_generation,
    40→    prepare_vace_generation_params
    41→)
    42→from ... import db_operations as db_ops
    43→from ...core.log import headless_logger, orchestrator_logger
    44→
    45→from .vace_quantization import _calculate_vace_quantization
    46→
    47→def handle_join_clips_task(
    48→    task_params_from_db: dict,
    49→    main_output_dir_base: Path,
    50→    task_id: str,
    51→    task_queue = None) -> Tuple[bool, str]:
    52→    """
    53→    Handle join_clips task: bridge two video clips using VACE generation.
    54→
    55→    Args:
    56→        task_params_from_db: Task parameters including:
    57→            - starting_video_path: Path to first video clip
    58→            - ending_video_path: Path to second video clip
    59→            - context_frame_count: Number of frames to extract from each clip
    60→            - gap_frame_count: Number of frames to generate between clips (INSERT mode) or replace (REPLACE mode)
    61→            - replace_mode: Optional bool (default False). If True, gap frames REPLACE boundary frames instead of being inserted
    62→            - prompt: Generation prompt for the transition
    63→            - aspect_ratio: Optional aspect ratio (e.g., "16:9", "9:16", "1:1") to standardize both videos
    64→            - model: Optional model override (defaults to wan_2_2_vace_lightning_baseline_2_2_2)
    65→            - resolution: Optional [width, height] override
    66→            - use_input_video_resolution: Optional bool (default False). If True, uses the detected resolution from input video instead of resolution override
    67→            - fps: Optional FPS override (defaults to 16)
    68→            - use_input_video_fps: Optional bool (default False). If True, uses input video's FPS. If False, downsamples to fps param (default 16)
    69→            - max_wait_time: Optional timeout in seconds for generation (defaults to 1800s / 30 minutes)
    70→            - additional_loras: Optional dict of additional LoRAs {name: weight}
    71→            - Other standard VACE parameters (guidance_scale, flow_shift, etc.)
    72→        main_output_dir_base: Base output directory
    73→        task_id: Task ID for logging and status updates
    74→        task_queue: HeadlessTaskQueue instance for generation
    75→
    76→    Returns:
    77→        Tuple of (success: bool, output_path_or_message: str)
    78→    """
    79→    orchestrator_logger.debug(f"[JOIN_CLIPS] Task {task_id}: Starting join_clips handler")
    80→
    81→    try:
    82→        # --- 1. Extract and Validate Parameters ---
    83→        starting_video_path = task_params_from_db.get("starting_video_path")
    84→        ending_video_path = task_params_from_db.get("ending_video_path")
    85→        context_frame_count = task_params_from_db.get("context_frame_count", 8)
    86→        gap_frame_count = task_params_from_db.get("gap_frame_count", 53)
    87→        replace_mode = task_params_from_db.get("replace_mode", False)  # If True, gap REPLACES frames instead of inserting
    88→        prompt = task_params_from_db.get("prompt", "")
    89→        aspect_ratio = task_params_from_db.get("aspect_ratio")  # Optional: e.g., "16:9", "9:16", "1:1"
    90→
    91→        # Extract keep_bridging_images param
    92→        keep_bridging_images = task_params_from_db.get("keep_bridging_images", False)
    93→
    94→        # transition_only mode: generate transition video without stitching
    95→        # Used by parallel join architecture where final stitch happens in a separate task
    96→        transition_only = task_params_from_db.get("transition_only", False)
    97→
    98→        orchestrator_logger.debug(f"[JOIN_CLIPS] Task {task_id}: Mode: {'REPLACE' if replace_mode else 'INSERT'}")
    99→        if transition_only:
   100→            orchestrator_logger.debug(f"[JOIN_CLIPS] Task {task_id}: transition_only=True - will output transition video only (no stitching)")
   101→        if replace_mode:
   102→            orchestrator_logger.debug(f"[JOIN_CLIPS] Task {task_id}: gap_frame_count={gap_frame_count} frames will REPLACE boundary frames (no insertion)")
   103→
   104→        # Check if this is part of an orchestrator and starting_video_path needs to be fetched
   105→        orchestrator_task_id_ref = task_params_from_db.get("orchestrator_task_id_ref")
   106→        is_first_join = task_params_from_db.get("is_first_join", False)
   107→        is_last_join = task_params_from_db.get("is_last_join", False)
   108→        audio_url = task_params_from_db.get("audio_url")  # Audio to add to final output (only used on last join)
   109→
   110→        if not starting_video_path:
   111→            # Check if this is an orchestrator child task that needs to fetch predecessor output
   112→            if orchestrator_task_id_ref:
   113→                if is_first_join:
   114→                    error_msg = "First join in orchestrator must have starting_video_path explicitly set"
   115→                    orchestrator_logger.debug(f"[JOIN_CLIPS_ERROR] Task {task_id}: {error_msg}")
   116→                    return False, error_msg
   117→
   118→                orchestrator_logger.debug(f"[JOIN_CLIPS] Task {task_id}: Part of orchestrator {orchestrator_task_id_ref}, fetching predecessor output")
   119→
   120→                # Fetch predecessor output using edge function
   121→                predecessor_id, predecessor_output = db_ops.get_predecessor_output_via_edge_function(task_id)
   122→
   123→                if not predecessor_output:
   124→                    error_msg = f"Failed to fetch predecessor output (predecessor_id={predecessor_id})"
   125→                    orchestrator_logger.debug(f"[JOIN_CLIPS_ERROR] Task {task_id}: {error_msg}")
   126→                    return False, error_msg
   127→
   128→                starting_video_path = predecessor_output
   129→                orchestrator_logger.debug(f"[JOIN_CLIPS] Task {task_id}: Fetched predecessor output: {predecessor_output}")
   130→            else:
   131→                # Standalone join_clips task without starting_video_path
   132→                error_msg = "starting_video_path is required"
   133→                orchestrator_logger.debug(f"[JOIN_CLIPS_ERROR] Task {task_id}: {error_msg}")
   134→                return False, error_msg
   135→
   136→        if not ending_video_path:
   137→            error_msg = "ending_video_path is required"
   138→            orchestrator_logger.debug(f"[JOIN_CLIPS_ERROR] Task {task_id}: {error_msg}")
   139→            return False, error_msg
   140→
   141→        # Validate task queue
   142→        if task_queue is None:
   143→            error_msg = "task_queue is required for join_clips"
   144→            orchestrator_logger.debug(f"[JOIN_CLIPS_ERROR] Task {task_id}: {error_msg}")
   145→            return False, error_msg
   146→
   147→        # Create working directory - use custom dir if provided by orchestrator
   148→        if "join_output_dir" in task_params_from_db:
   149→            join_clips_dir = Path(task_params_from_db["join_output_dir"])
   150→            orchestrator_logger.debug(f"[JOIN_CLIPS] Task {task_id}: Using orchestrator output dir: {join_clips_dir}")
   151→        else:
   152→            join_clips_dir = main_output_dir_base / "join_clips" / task_id
   153→
   154→        join_clips_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
   155→
   156→        # Download videos if they are URLs (e.g., Supabase storage URLs)
   157→        orchestrator_logger.debug(f"[JOIN_CLIPS] Task {task_id}: Checking if videos need to be downloaded...")
   158→        starting_video_path = download_video_if_url(
   159→            starting_video_path,
   160→            download_target_dir=join_clips_dir,
   161→            task_id_for_logging=task_id,
   162→            descriptive_name="starting_video"

   164→        ending_video_path = download_video_if_url(

   170→
   171→        # Convert to Path objects and validate existence

   175→        if not starting_video.exists():

   209→        # --- 2a. Ensure Videos are at Target FPS ---

   219→            target_fps_param = task_params_from_db.get("fps") or 16

   264→        # --- 2b. Standardize Videos to Target Aspect Ratio (if specified) ---
   265→        if aspect_ratio:

   267→
   268→            # Standardize starting video

   270→            result = standardize_video_aspect_ratio(

   275→            if result is None:

   278→                return False, error_msg
   279→            starting_video = standardized_start_path

   281→
   282→            # Standardize ending video
   283→            standardized_end_path = join_clips_dir / f"end_standardized_{task_id}.mp4"
   284→            result = standardize_video_aspect_ratio(
   285→                input_video_path=ending_video,

   289→            if result is None:
   290→                error_msg = f"Failed to standardize ending video to aspect ratio {aspect_ratio}"

   292→                return False, error_msg
   293→            ending_video = standardized_end_path
   294→            orchestrator_logger.debug(f"[JOIN_CLIPS] Task {task_id}: Ending video standardized")
   295→        else:
   296→            # No explicit aspect ratio specified - auto-standardize ending video to match starting video
   297→            orchestrator_logger.debug(f"[JOIN_CLIPS] Task {task_id}: Checking if videos have matching aspect ratios...")
   298→
   299→            # Get dimensions of both videos
   300→            try:
   301→                import subprocess
   302→
   303→                def get_video_dimensions(video_path):
   304→                    probe_cmd = [
   305→                        'ffprobe', '-v', 'error',
   306→                        '-select_streams', 'v:0',
   307→                        '-show_entries', 'stream=width,height',
   308→                        '-of', 'csv=p=0',
   309→                        str(video_path)
   310→                    ]
   311→                    result = subprocess.run(probe_cmd, capture_output=True, text=True, timeout=10)
   312→                    if result.returncode != 0:
   313→                        return None, None
   314→                    width_str, height_str = result.stdout.strip().split(',')
   315→                    return int(width_str), int(height_str)
   316→
   317→                start_w, start_h = get_video_dimensions(starting_video)
   318→                end_w, end_h = get_video_dimensions(ending_video)
   319→
   320→                if start_w and start_h and end_w and end_h:
   321→                    start_aspect = start_w / start_h
   322→                    end_aspect = end_w / end_h
   323→
   324→                    orchestrator_logger.debug(f"[JOIN_CLIPS] Task {task_id}: Starting video: {start_w}x{start_h} (aspect: {start_aspect:.3f})")
   325→                    orchestrator_logger.debug(f"[JOIN_CLIPS] Task {task_id}: Ending video: {end_w}x{end_h} (aspect: {end_aspect:.3f})")
   326→
   327→                    # If aspect ratios differ by more than 1%, standardize ending video to match starting video
   328→                    if abs(start_aspect - end_aspect) > 0.01:
   329→                        orchestrator_logger.debug(f"[JOIN_CLIPS] Task {task_id}: Aspect ratios differ - standardizing ending video to match starting video")
   330→
   331→                        # Calculate aspect ratio string from starting video
   332→                        # Use common aspect ratios or create from dimensions
   333→                        if abs(start_aspect - 16/9) < 0.01:
   334→                            auto_aspect_ratio = "16:9"
   335→                        elif abs(start_aspect - 9/16) < 0.01:
   336→                            auto_aspect_ratio = "9:16"
   337→                        elif abs(start_aspect - 1.0) < 0.01:
   338→                            auto_aspect_ratio = "1:1"
   339→                        elif abs(start_aspect - 4/3) < 0.01:
   340→                            auto_aspect_ratio = "4:3"
   341→                        elif abs(start_aspect - 21/9) < 0.01:
   342→                            auto_aspect_ratio = "21:9"

   348→
   349→                        standardized_end_path = join_clips_dir / f"end_standardized_{task_id}.mp4"
   350→                        result = standardize_video_aspect_ratio(

   363→            except (OSError, ValueError, RuntimeError) as e:

   367→        # --- 3. Extract Video Properties ---
   368→        try:
   369→            start_frame_count, start_fps = get_video_frame_count_and_fps(str(starting_video))

   371→
   372→            orchestrator_logger.debug(f"[JOIN_CLIPS] Task {task_id}: Starting video - {start_frame_count} frames @ {start_fps} fps")
   373→            orchestrator_logger.debug(f"[JOIN_CLIPS] Task {task_id}: Ending video - {end_frame_count} frames @ {end_fps} fps")
   374→
   375→        except (OSError, ValueError, RuntimeError) as e:
   376→            error_msg = f"Failed to extract video properties: {e}"
   377→            orchestrator_logger.debug(f"[JOIN_CLIPS_ERROR] Task {task_id}: {error_msg}")
   378→            return False, error_msg
   379→
   380→        # Validate that frame counts were detected (WebM and some codecs may fail)
   381→        if start_frame_count is None:
   382→            error_msg = f"Could not detect frame count for starting video: {starting_video}. The video may be corrupt, empty, or in an unsupported format."
   383→            orchestrator_logger.debug(f"[JOIN_CLIPS_ERROR] Task {task_id}: {error_msg}")
   384→            return False, error_msg
   385→
   386→        if end_frame_count is None:
   387→            error_msg = f"Could not detect frame count for ending video: {ending_video}. The video may be corrupt, empty, or in an unsupported format."
   388→            orchestrator_logger.debug(f"[JOIN_CLIPS_ERROR] Task {task_id}: {error_msg}")
   389→            return False, error_msg
   390→
   391→        # Auto-adjust context frame count if it exceeds available frames
   392→        original_context_frame_count = context_frame_count
   393→        max_available_context = min(start_frame_count, end_frame_count)
   394→
   395→        if context_frame_count > max_available_context:
   396→            context_frame_count = max_available_context
   397→            orchestrator_logger.debug(f"[JOIN_CLIPS] Task {task_id}: \u26a0\ufe0f Auto-adjusted context_frame_count from {original_context_frame_count} to {context_frame_count} (limited by shortest video: start={start_frame_count}, end={end_frame_count})")
   398→            headless_logger.warning(
   399→                f"[JOIN_CLIPS] Task {task_id}: context_frame_count reduced from {original_context_frame_count} to {context_frame_count} to fit available frames",
   400→                task_id=task_id
   401→            )
   402→
   403→        # Set target FPS based on use_input_video_fps setting
   404→        if use_input_video_fps:

   407→        else:
   408→            target_fps = task_params_from_db.get("fps") or 16
   409→            orchestrator_logger.debug(f"[JOIN_CLIPS] Task {task_id}: Using target FPS: {target_fps}")
   410→
   411→        # --- 4. Calculate gap sizes first (needed for REPLACE mode context extraction) ---
   412→        # Calculate VACE quantization adjustments early so we know exact gap sizes
   413→        quantization_result = _calculate_vace_quantization(
   414→            context_frame_count=context_frame_count,
   415→            gap_frame_count=gap_frame_count,
   416→            replace_mode=replace_mode
   417→        )
   418→        gap_for_guide = quantization_result['gap_for_guide']
   419→        quantization_shift = quantization_result['quantization_shift']
   420→
   421→        # Calculate gap split for REPLACE mode
   422→        # Start with even split, then adjust if one clip is too short
   423→        gap_from_clip1 = gap_for_guide // 2 if replace_mode else 0
   424→        gap_from_clip2 = (gap_for_guide - gap_from_clip1) if replace_mode else 0
   425→
   426→        orchestrator_logger.debug(f"[JOIN_CLIPS] Task {task_id}: Mode={'REPLACE' if replace_mode else 'INSERT'}, gap_for_guide={gap_for_guide}")
   427→        if replace_mode:
   428→            orchestrator_logger.debug(f"[JOIN_CLIPS] Task {task_id}: REPLACE initial split: {gap_from_clip1} from clip1, {gap_from_clip2} from clip2")
   429→
   430→            # Dynamically adjust gap split if one clip is too short
   431→            # Each clip needs: gap_from_clip + at least 1 frame for context
   432→            min_frames_needed_clip1 = gap_from_clip1 + 1
   433→            _min_frames_needed_clip2 = gap_from_clip2 + 1
   434→
   435→            if start_frame_count < min_frames_needed_clip1:
   436→                # Clip1 too short - shift gap frames to clip2
   437→                max_gap_from_clip1 = max(0, start_frame_count - 1)  # Leave at least 1 frame for context
   438→                shift_amount = gap_from_clip1 - max_gap_from_clip1
   439→                gap_from_clip1 = max_gap_from_clip1
   440→                gap_from_clip2 = gap_from_clip2 + shift_amount
   441→                orchestrator_logger.debug(f"[JOIN_CLIPS] Task {task_id}: \u26a0\ufe0f Clip1 too short ({start_frame_count} frames) - shifted {shift_amount} gap frames to clip2")
   442→                orchestrator_logger.debug(f"[JOIN_CLIPS] Task {task_id}: Adjusted split: {gap_from_clip1} from clip1, {gap_from_clip2} from clip2")
   443→
   444→            if end_frame_count < gap_from_clip2 + 1:
   445→                # Clip2 too short - shift gap frames to clip1
   446→                max_gap_from_clip2 = max(0, end_frame_count - 1)
   447→                shift_amount = gap_from_clip2 - max_gap_from_clip2
   448→                gap_from_clip2 = max_gap_from_clip2
   449→                gap_from_clip1 = gap_from_clip1 + shift_amount
   450→                orchestrator_logger.debug(f"[JOIN_CLIPS] Task {task_id}: \u26a0\ufe0f Clip2 too short ({end_frame_count} frames) - shifted {shift_amount} gap frames to clip1")
   451→                orchestrator_logger.debug(f"[JOIN_CLIPS] Task {task_id}: Adjusted split: {gap_from_clip1} from clip1, {gap_from_clip2} from clip2")
   452→
   453→            # Final validation - check if total gap is still achievable
   454→            total_available = (start_frame_count - 1) + (end_frame_count - 1)  # -1 for minimum 1 context frame each
   455→            if gap_for_guide > total_available:
   456→                error_msg = (
   457→                    f"Videos too short for requested gap: need {gap_for_guide} gap frames but only "
   458→                    f"{total_available} available (start: {start_frame_count}, end: {end_frame_count}). "
   459→                    f"Try reducing gap_frame_count or using longer source clips."
   460→                )
   461→                orchestrator_logger.debug(f"[JOIN_CLIPS_ERROR] Task {task_id}: {error_msg}")
   462→                return False, error_msg
   463→        orchestrator_logger.debug(
   464→            f"[JOIN_CLIPS] Task {task_id}: gap split summary: "
   465→            f"requested_gap={gap_frame_count}, gap_for_guide={gap_for_guide}, "
   466→            f"gap_from_clip1={gap_from_clip1}, gap_from_clip2={gap_from_clip2}, "
   467→            f"context_frame_count={context_frame_count}"
   468→        )
   469→
   470→        # --- 5. Extract Context Frames ---
   471→        orchestrator_logger.debug(f"[JOIN_CLIPS] Task {task_id}: Extracting context frames...")
   472→
   473→        try:
   474→            # Extract all frames from both videos
   475→            start_all_frames = extract_frames_from_video(str(starting_video))
   476→            if not start_all_frames or len(start_all_frames) < context_frame_count:
   477→                error_msg = f"Failed to extract {context_frame_count} frames from starting video"
   478→                orchestrator_logger.debug(f"[JOIN_CLIPS_ERROR] Task {task_id}: {error_msg}")
   479→                return False, error_msg
   480→
   481→            end_all_frames = extract_frames_from_video(str(ending_video))
   482→            if not end_all_frames or len(end_all_frames) < context_frame_count:
   483→                error_msg = f"Failed to extract {context_frame_count} frames from ending video"
   484→                orchestrator_logger.debug(f"[JOIN_CLIPS_ERROR] Task {task_id}: {error_msg}")
   485→                return False, error_msg
   486→
   487→            # Initialize vid2vid source video path (will be set in REPLACE mode if enabled)
   488→            vid2vid_source_video_path = None
   489→
   490→            if replace_mode:
   491→                # REPLACE mode: Context comes from OUTSIDE the gap region
   492→                # Gap is removed from boundary, context is adjacent to (but outside) the gap
   493→                #
   494→                # clip1: [...][context 8][gap N removed]
   495→                # clip2: [gap M removed][context 8][...]
   496→                #
   497→                clip1_available = len(start_all_frames)
   498→                clip2_available = len(end_all_frames)
   499→                min_clip_frames = min(clip1_available, clip2_available)
   500→

1→"""Resolution parsing and snapping utilities."""
     2→
     3→__all__ = [
     4→    "snap_resolution_to_model_grid",
     5→    "parse_resolution",
     6→]
     7→
     8→
     9→def snap_resolution_to_model_grid(parsed_res: tuple[int, int], grid_size: int = 16) -> tuple[int, int]:
    10→    """
    11→    Snaps resolution to model grid requirements.
    12→
    13→    Args:
    14→        parsed_res: (width, height) tuple
    15→        grid_size: Grid alignment size in pixels (16 for Wan, 64 for LTX-2)
    16→
    17→    Returns:
    18→        (width, height) tuple snapped to nearest valid values
    19→    """
    20→    width, height = parsed_res
    21→    width = (width // grid_size) * grid_size
    22→    height = (height // grid_size) * grid_size
    23→    return width, height
    24→
    25→
    26→def get_model_grid_size(model_name: str | None) -> int:
    27→    """Get the VAE block size / grid alignment for a model.
    28→
    29→    Queries WGP's model handler when available, falls back to 16 (Wan default).
    30→    """
    31→    if not model_name:
    32→        return 16
    33→    try:
    34→        from wgp import get_model_def
    35→        model_def = get_model_def(model_name)
    36→        if model_def is None:
    37→            return 16
    38→        # Check model family handler for vae_block_size
    39→        family = model_def.get("family")
    40→        if family == "ltx2":
    41→            return 64
    42→    except (ImportError, TypeError, ValueError, KeyError, AttributeError):
    43→        pass
    44→    # Fallback: check model name heuristically
    45→    if model_name and "ltx2" in model_name.lower():
    46→        return 64
    47→    return 16
    48→
    49→
    50→def parse_resolution(res_str: str) -> tuple[int, int]:
    51→    """Parses 'WIDTHxHEIGHT' string to (width, height) tuple."""
    52→    try:
    53→        w, h = map(int, res_str.split('x'))
    54→        if w <= 0 or h <= 0:
    55→            raise ValueError("Width and height must be positive.")
    56→        return w, h
    57→    except ValueError as e:
    58→        raise ValueError(f"Resolution string must be in WIDTHxHEIGHT format with positive integers (e.g., '960x544'), got {res_str}. Error: {e}") from e
    59→

22→def add_task_to_db(task_payload: dict, task_type_str: str, dependant_on: str | list[str] | None = None, db_path: str | None = None) -> str:
    23→    """
    24→    Adds a new task to the Supabase database via Edge Function.
    25→
    26→    Args:
    27→        task_payload: Task parameters dictionary
    28→        task_type_str: Type of task being created
    29→        dependant_on: Optional dependency - single task ID string or list of task IDs.
    30→                      When list is provided, task will only run when ALL dependencies complete.
    31→        db_path: Ignored (kept for API compatibility)
    32→
    33→    Returns:
    34→        str: The database row ID (UUID) assigned to the task
    35→    """
    36→    # Generate a new UUID for the database row ID
    37→    import uuid
    38→    actual_db_row_id = str(uuid.uuid4())
    39→
    40→    # Sanitize payload and get project_id
    41→    params_for_db = task_payload.copy()
    42→    params_for_db.pop("task_type", None)  # Ensure task_type is not duplicated in params
    43→    project_id = task_payload.get("project_id", "default_project_id")
    44→
    45→    # Build Edge URL
    46→    edge_url = (
    47→        _cfg.SUPABASE_EDGE_CREATE_TASK_URL
    48→    ) or os.getenv("SUPABASE_EDGE_CREATE_TASK_URL") or (
    49→        f"{_cfg.SUPABASE_URL.rstrip('/')}/functions/v1/create-task" if _cfg.SUPABASE_URL else None
    50→    )
    51→
    52→    if not edge_url:
    53→        raise ValueError("Edge Function URL for create-task is not configured")
    54→
    55→    headers = {"Content-Type": "application/json"}
    56→    if _cfg.SUPABASE_ACCESS_TOKEN:
    57→        headers["Authorization"] = f"Bearer {_cfg.SUPABASE_ACCESS_TOKEN}"
    58→
    59→    # Normalize dependant_on to list format for consistency
    60→    # Edge Function expects: null, or JSON array of UUIDs
    61→    dependant_on_list: list[str] | None = None
    62→    if dependant_on:
    63→        if isinstance(dependant_on, str):
    64→            dependant_on_list = [dependant_on]
    65→        else:
    66→            dependant_on_list = list(dependant_on)
    67→
    68→    # Defensive check: if dependencies are specified, ensure they all exist before enqueueing
    69→    if dependant_on_list:
    70→        max_retries = 3
    71→        retry_delay = 0.5

### **1. UPLOAD FUNCTIONS** (`source/utils/output_path_utils.py`)

```python
def prepare_output_path_with_upload(
    task_id: str,
    filename: str,
    main_output_dir_base: Path,
    task_type: str | None = None,
    *,
    custom_output_dir: str | Path | None = None
) -> tuple[Path, str]:
```

**Returns:** `(local_save_path, initial_db_location)` where:
- `local_save_path`: Path object where file should be saved locally
- `initial_db_location`: Path string for database storage (local path or URL after upload)

**Key behavior:**
- Sanitizes filename via `sanitize_filename_for_storage()` to remove S3/Supabase-unsafe characters
- Uses task type subdirectory structure if `task_type` provided
- Returns local path first; actual Supabase upload now handled by edge function

#### `upload_intermediate_file_to_storage(local_file_path, task_id, filename)`

```python
def upload_intermediate_file_to_storage(
    local_file_path: Path,
    task_id: str,
    filename: str
) -> str | None:
```

**Returns:** Public Supabase URL on success, or `None` on failure

**Key features:**

- Includes retry logic (3 attempts) for transient failures (502/503/504, timeouts)
- Constructs public URL: `{SUPABASE_URL}/storage/v1/object/public/image_uploads/{storage_path}`
- Checks `SUPABASE_URL` and `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY` / `SUPABASE_ANON_KEY` from environment

#### `sanitize_filename_for_storage(filename)`
**Location:** Lines 123-157

**Removes unsafe characters for S3/Supabase:**
- Unicode special chars: `§®©™·º½¾¿¡`
- Control chars: `0x00-0x1F`, `0x7F-0x9F`
- Path separators and problematic chars: `<>:"/\|?*,`
- Returns default `"sanitized_file"` if result is empty
