Now I have a thorough understanding of the entire codebase. Let me compile the full implementation plan.

---

## Implementation Plan: Lift Clip Preprocessing to Join Clips Orchestrator

### 1. Problem Summary

In the parallel join architecture, `join_clips_segment` tasks preprocess clips (FPS normalization + aspect ratio cropping) before generating transitions. The transition metadata records frame counts and indices against the preprocessed clips. However, `join_final_stitch` downloads the **raw original** clips from `clip_list` and tries to use those frame indices -- causing crashes when the original clips have different FPS or aspect ratios than what the segments preprocessed to.

### 2. Solution Overview

Create a new `clip_preprocessor.py` module that runs **in the orchestrator** after `clip_list` is resolved but **before** validation, VLM enhancement, and child task creation. This module:
1. Downloads each clip
2. Normalizes FPS (unless `use_input_video_fps=True`)
3. Standardizes aspect ratio (explicit or auto-detected from first clip)
4. Detects resolution from first clip (if `use_input_video_resolution=True`)
5. Validates frame counts (folding in `clip_validator.py` functionality)
6. Uploads preprocessed clips to Supabase
7. Returns a new `clip_list` with preprocessed URLs, detected resolution, and frame counts

The updated `clip_list` then flows through `task_builder.py` to both segments and final stitch, so both sides see identical preprocessed clips.

---

### 3. New File: `source/task_handlers/join/clip_preprocessor.py`

**Location:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/Headless-Wan2GP/source/task_handlers/join/clip_preprocessor.py`

**Pattern follows:** `clip_validator.py` (same directory, similar purpose, same logging style)

#### 3.1 Function Signature

```python
"""
Clip preprocessing for join operations.

Downloads clips, normalizes FPS, standardizes aspect ratios, validates frame counts,
and uploads preprocessed clips to Supabase for cross-worker access.

This runs in the orchestrator so both segment tasks and final_stitch receive
identical preprocessed clip URLs, preventing frame count mismatches.
"""

from dataclasses import dataclass, field
from pathlib import Path
from typing import Tuple, List, Optional

from source.utils import download_video_if_url, upload_intermediate_file_to_storage
from source.utils import get_video_frame_count_and_fps
from source.media.video import ensure_video_fps, standardize_video_aspect_ratio
from source.core.log import orchestrator_logger

__all__ = [
    "PreprocessedClipResult",
    "preprocess_clips_for_join",
]


@dataclass
class PreprocessedClipResult:
    """Result of preprocessing all clips for a join operation."""
    clip_list: List[dict]  # Updated clip_list with preprocessed URLs
    frame_counts: List[int]  # Frame counts of preprocessed clips
    detected_resolution: Optional[Tuple[int, int]]  # (width, height) if detected
    detected_aspect_ratio: Optional[str]  # e.g., "16:9" if auto-detected
    validation_message: str  # Warning message from frame validation, or ""


def preprocess_clips_for_join(
    clip_list: List[dict],
    join_settings: dict,
    orchestrator_task_id: str,
    work_dir: Path,
) -> Tuple[bool, str | PreprocessedClipResult]:
    """
    Preprocess all clips: download, normalize FPS, standardize aspect ratio,
    validate frames, upload, and return updated clip_list.
    
    This consolidates what was previously 3 separate orchestrator steps:
    1. Frame validation (clip_validator.py)
    2. Resolution detection (_get_video_resolution)
    3. FPS/aspect normalization (done redundantly in each segment)
    
    Args:
        clip_list: List of clip dicts with 'url' and optional 'name' keys
        join_settings: Join settings dict (from _extract_join_settings_from_payload)
            Relevant keys: fps, use_input_video_fps, aspect_ratio,
            use_input_video_resolution, gap_frame_count, context_frame_count, replace_mode
        orchestrator_task_id: Task ID for logging and upload paths
        work_dir: Working directory for downloads and temp files
        
    Returns:
        (True, PreprocessedClipResult) on success
        (False, error_message: str) on failure
    """
```

#### 3.2 Internal Flow

The function body follows this sequence:

**Step 1: Create subdirectory**
```python
preprocess_dir = work_dir / "preprocess"
preprocess_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
```

**Step 2: Download all clips**
- Loop through `clip_list`, call `download_video_if_url()` for each
- Store local paths in a list
- If any download fails, return `(False, "Failed to download clip N: ...")`

**Step 3: Detect resolution from first clip (if use_input_video_resolution)**
- Use ffprobe (same pattern as `_get_video_resolution` in orchestrator.py) on the first downloaded clip
- Store `detected_resolution` for inclusion in result

**Step 4: Determine target aspect ratio**
- If `join_settings["aspect_ratio"]` is set explicitly, use it as `target_aspect_ratio`
- Otherwise, auto-detect from first clip using the same common-ratio matching logic from generation.py lines 296-365:
  - Get dimensions of first clip via ffprobe
  - Compute aspect ratio
  - Map to closest standard ratio string ("16:9", "9:16", "1:1", "4:3", "21:9") or use exact "{w}:{h}"
  - Store as `detected_aspect_ratio`

**Step 5: Normalize FPS (unless use_input_video_fps=True)**
- Extract `use_input_video_fps` from `join_settings`
- If `False`: determine `target_fps = join_settings.get("fps") or 16`
- Loop through local clip paths, call `ensure_video_fps(clip_path, target_fps, preprocess_dir)` for each
- Update local paths list with resampled paths (ensure_video_fps returns original if already correct)
- If `True`: skip this step entirely, log it

**Step 6: Standardize aspect ratio**
- If `target_aspect_ratio` was determined (step 4):
  - For each clip, check if its aspect ratio already matches (within 1% tolerance) using ffprobe
  - If mismatch: call `standardize_video_aspect_ratio(clip_path, output_path, target_aspect_ratio, orchestrator_task_id)`
  - Update local paths list
- If no target_aspect_ratio determined and only 1 clip differs, standardize per-pair as safety net

**Step 7: Validate frame counts (folded from clip_validator.py)**
- Call `get_video_frame_count_and_fps()` on each preprocessed clip
- Apply the same validation logic from `validate_clip_frames_for_join`:
  - Calculate `min_frames` using `calculate_min_clip_frames()`
  - Check first/last clip boundary requirements
  - Produce warnings for proportional reduction if needed
- Store `frame_counts` list
- If insufficient frames: return `(False, "Clip frame validation failed: ...")`

**Step 8: Upload preprocessed clips to Supabase**
- For each clip where local path differs from original (i.e., was actually preprocessed):
  - Call `upload_intermediate_file_to_storage(local_path, orchestrator_task_id, filename)`
  - If upload succeeds: use public URL in new clip_list
  - If upload fails: use local path with warning (same pattern as loop_first_clip at line 176 of orchestrator.py)
- For clips that were not modified (already at correct FPS and aspect ratio): keep original URL
- Build new `clip_list` with updated URLs

**Step 9: Return result**
```python
return True, PreprocessedClipResult(
    clip_list=new_clip_list,
    frame_counts=frame_counts,
    detected_resolution=detected_resolution,
    detected_aspect_ratio=detected_aspect_ratio,
    validation_message=validation_message,
)
```

#### 3.3 Helper: `_detect_aspect_ratio_from_video`

Extract the common-ratio detection logic from generation.py into a standalone helper within this module:

```python
def _detect_aspect_ratio_from_video(video_path: Path, task_id: str) -> Optional[str]:
    """
    Detect the aspect ratio of a video and return as a standard ratio string.
    
    Returns "16:9", "9:16", "1:1", "4:3", "21:9", or "{w}:{h}" for non-standard.
    Returns None if detection fails.
    """
```

This uses the `_get_video_resolution` pattern (ffprobe) then maps to standard ratios using the same tolerance logic from generation.py lines 333-345.

#### 3.4 Helper: `_get_video_resolution`

Move (or duplicate) the resolution detection from orchestrator.py as a private helper:

```python
def _get_video_resolution(video_path: str | Path) -> Tuple[int, int] | None:
    """Get video resolution (width, height) using ffprobe."""
```

This is identical to `orchestrator.py:_get_video_resolution` -- we can import from orchestrator or inline. I recommend inlining since the orchestrator's version will be removed.

**REMOVE: Block 1 -- Frame validation** (lines 210-233)

    ...validate_clip_frames_for_join(...)...
```

```

**REMOVE: `_get_video_resolution` function** (lines 41-64) -- moved to clip_preprocessor.py

**ADD: Single preprocessing call** (inserted where Block 1 was):
```python
# === PREPROCESS CLIPS (FPS normalize, aspect ratio, validate, upload) ===
skip_validation = orchestrator_payload.get("skip_frame_validation", False)
preprocess_result = preprocess_clips_for_join(
    clip_list=clip_list,
    join_settings=join_settings,
    orchestrator_task_id=orchestrator_task_id_str,
    work_dir=current_run_output_dir,
)

if isinstance(preprocess_result, tuple):
    success, result_or_msg = preprocess_result
    if not success:
        orchestrator_logger.debug(f"[JOIN_ORCHESTRATOR] PREPROCESSING FAILED: {result_or_msg}")
        return False, f"Clip preprocessing failed: {result_or_msg}"
    
    # Update clip_list with preprocessed URLs
    clip_list = result_or_msg.clip_list
    
    # Apply detected resolution if use_input_video_resolution was requested
    if result_or_msg.detected_resolution and join_settings.get("use_input_video_resolution", False):
        join_settings["resolution"] = list(result_or_msg.detected_resolution)
        orchestrator_logger.debug(f"[JOIN_ORCHESTRATOR] Detected resolution: {result_or_msg.detected_resolution}")
    
    if result_or_msg.validation_message:
        orchestrator_logger.debug(f"[JOIN_ORCHESTRATOR] {result_or_msg.validation_message}")
    
    orchestrator_logger.debug(f"[JOIN_ORCHESTRATOR] Preprocessing complete, frame counts: {result_or_msg.frame_counts}")
```
